I built to the design system already in the repo instead — Amezo orange `#f4750f`, Public Sans, the rounded-full search pill, existing `Card`/`Badge`/`RatingBadge` primitives — so the visual language is consistent, but specific spacing, copy and section order from your files will need a pass. Everything structural below is design-independent and won't need redoing.

## What shipped

**Reusable chrome** — `SiteHeader` + `SiteFooter`, rendered once from a new `BuyerLayout` route rather than per page. `AppHeader` is deleted; `ProductDetail`, `Checkout`, `OrderConfirmation` and `SearchResults` no longer each mount their own header with their own `onSearch`.

One design decision worth flagging: pages under a layout route can't pass props to the header, so the header reads the query off the URL itself. Searching from `/search` keeps the filters you already applied and restarts paging; searching from anywhere else starts clean.

**Landing page at `/`** — hero with a marketplace search box and a spotlight on the best-rated *in-stock* product, category tiles, "Trending this week" and "New arrivals" rails, a why-buy-here block, and a seller CTA. Rails read the same `/products` endpoint search does (there's no merchandising endpoint), each a small pre-sorted, cached page of it.

**Search moved to `/search`**, and old links carrying a query to `/` are forwarded to `/search` rather than dropping the term.

Footer links only point at routes the app actually serves — no placeholder hrefs.

## Verification

94 unit tests pass (14 new), 4 Playwright specs pass in a real browser, lint unchanged at the same 5 pre-existing warnings, production build clean.

Two notes on the container: the e2e suite can't run as-is here — Playwright 1.63 pins Chromium 1243 and the image ships 1194 — so I ran it through a throwaway config pointed at the installed binary and deleted that config. That affects this container, not CI. Also, `pnpm run gen:api` had never been run, so `src/lib/api/schema.d.ts` was missing and typecheck was failing repo-wide before I touched anything; generating it fixed ~25 pre-existing errors. It's gitignored, so nothing to commit.

One small change beyond strict scope: Checkout's "Back to cart" link pointed at `/`, which now means the landing page rather than browse. I relabeled it "Continue shopping" and pointed it at `/search`. Easy to revert if you'd rather it opened the cart drawer.

Got the designs — thanks. Pushed as `22611c4`.

`github.md` confirmed the design was built from this repo's tokens, so everything mapped straight across: `#f4750f`, Public Sans, 8/12px radii, the 1320px shell.

## Built

**Landing** — promo hero with working carousel dots, circular category rail, four product rails split by the three promo tiles and the search band. **Header** — delivery location, labelled cart, and the second row with category nav and Best deals. **Footer** — the design's compact single row. **New `/stores/:brand` storefront** — cover, seller header, stats, category chips, sort — reached from the product page's new "Sold by" line and the brand crumb.

`ProductCard` folded into a single `ProductTile`, so a product now looks identical on the rails, in search, and on a storefront.

## Where I didn't follow the design

The design shows things the API can't back. I built the markup but left it data-driven rather than faked:

- **Hero campaign** — it advertises "iPhone 16 Pro Max, from $1,199". There's no campaign API and the catalog has no iPhone, so each slide is a real listing with its real title, price and link.
- **Discount flashes** — `-25%` badges and struck-through was-prices. Nothing carries a compare-at price. `ProductTile` accepts one and renders neither until the API supplies it. Inventing a discount on a real listing isn't a styling detail, so I wouldn't hardcode these.
- **Rail titles and promo tiles** — "Today's best deals", "60% off winter wear", a clearance push. They now name what they actually link to.
- **The AI band** — shipped as search, because that's what it does. The "Amezo AI / AI-powered shopping experience" copy is one string in `SearchBanner.tsx` whenever something AI sits behind it. Your call — say the word and I'll put it back.
- **Left out entirely rather than shipped inert:** Follow/Message on the storefront, seller policies (returns/warranty/ships-from), the LIVE badge, and buyer "Sign in" — that slot shows the magic-link identity when checkout established one, otherwise "Sell on Amezo". Footer's Help centre / Returns / Track order need pages that don't exist. All quick once there's something behind them.

Delivery city is real — a persisted localStorage preference, not a dead control.

Two things I changed on my own judgement: the landing page gained an `sr-only` h1 (the design's layout has no room for one, and the site's home page needs it), and rail grids use `auto-fill` with a 2-up mobile fallback — the design's `auto-fit` stretched a 1-item rail into a single 1300px-wide card, and on a phone made the page 10,000px tall.

## One soft spot

`useStoreProducts` scans a catalog page and filters by brand client-side, because `GET /products` has no seller or brand filter. Fine against fixtures, won't survive a real catalog — it wants a server-side `?sellerId=`. Flagged in the code.

## Verification

103 unit tests (25 new) and 5 Playwright specs pass, lint unchanged at the same 5 pre-existing warnings, build clean, both pages checked at 1440px and 390px.

Before coding
First, inspect the existing application, architecture, API patterns, authentication, routing, shared components, and the new designs.
Then create a plan covering:

* Screens and user flows to implement.
* Required frontend state and interactions.
* Required API endpoints and data needed by each screen.
* Proposed request/response shapes.
* Loading, empty, error, and success states.
* Any backend changes that will eventually be required.
* Reusable components that should be introduced.
* Any inconsistencies or ambiguities in the designs.

For APIs, design the contract first rather than inventing ad-hoc endpoints while implementing the UI.
Follow REST conventions:

* Use resource-oriented nouns, not action-based URLs.
* Use plural resource names for collections.
* Use appropriate HTTP methods (`GET`, `POST`, `PATCH`, `DELETE`, etc.).
* Use query parameters for filtering, sorting, pagination, and search where appropriate.
* Keep resource nesting shallow and meaningful.
* Use consistent request/response structures.
* Use consistent HTTP status codes and error responses.
* Version the API explicitly, for example `/api/v1/...`.
* Only introduce a new major API version for breaking changes.

The API contract should be something the backend developer can review and agree to before implementing the backend.
Important
Do not use fake/mock data just to make the screens look complete.
Instead, build the frontend around the proposed API contract and create the API client/types/hooks that the backend will eventually satisfy.
If an API does not exist yet, the frontend can temporarily have an explicit integration boundary so the screen can be developed and tested without hiding the missing backend implementation.

I have what I need. Here's the plan — no code written yet.

## Context

Main pulled (`9abdf03`), branch restarted from it (PR #7 merged). The zip carries 10 new screens; `Landing`, `Product Details` and `Store` are already built and unchanged.

`docs/api-design.md` already specifies `GET /orders` + `GET /orders/{orderId}` for buyer history — unbuilt, so My Orders lands on an agreed contract.

---

## 1. Screens

**Buyer (3)** — `My Orders`, `Order Detail`, `Refund Request`
**Seller (6)** — `Seller Dashboard`, `Seller Products` (rich rewrite), `Product Form`, `Seller Orders` (rich rewrite), `Seller Refunds`, `Store Settings`
**Ops (1)** — `Amezo Portal` — logistics console, **a third persona**

`Product Form` and `Order Detail` aren't pages — both are `$preview: {width: 640}` prop-driven components, i.e. drawer contents for their parent lists.

## 2. Main flows

- Buyer: orders list → filter/search → expand order → track timeline → request refund → watch refund state
- Seller: dashboard → low-stock/ship queues → product CRUD in drawer → order → pack → ship → refund queue → approve/decline → store profile
- Ops: parcel queue by state → scan history → manual state override / exception logging

## 3. API contract (proposed, `/api/v1`)

| Resource | Endpoints |
|---|---|
| Buyer orders | `GET /orders?status&from&to&q&page&size`, `GET /orders/{id}` |
| Refunds | `POST /refund-requests`, `GET /refund-requests`, `GET /refund-requests/{id}`, `PATCH /refund-requests/{id}` (seller decision), `GET /sellers/me/refund-requests?status` |
| Store | `GET|PATCH /sellers/me/store`, `GET /stores/{handle}`, `GET /stores/{handle}/products` |
| Seller products | `GET /sellers/me/products?q&status&category&sort&page&size`, `status` on `PATCH /products/{id}` |
| Seller orders | `GET /sellers/me/orders?q&status&sort&page&size`, `PATCH /sellers/me/orders/{id}` `{status, trackingNumber, parcels}` |
| Metrics | `GET /sellers/me/metrics?from&to&interval`, `GET /sellers/me/metrics/top-products` |
| Ops | `GET /parcels?state&hub&q`, `GET /parcels/{id}`, `POST /parcels/{id}/scans`, `PATCH /parcels/{id}` |

State machines live on the resource via `PATCH`, not action URLs. That means **replacing the existing `POST /sellers/me/orders/{id}/ship`**, which is the action-URL shape you're asking me to avoid.

`GET /stores/{handle}/products` also kills the client-side brand filter I flagged in PR #7.

## 4. Frontend architecture

New features: `features/orders`, `features/refunds`, `features/store-settings`, `features/seller-metrics`, `features/ops`.

Reusable components to introduce: `DataTable` (sort/filter/paginate — three screens need it), `Drawer` (on existing `sheet.tsx`), `StatusBadge`, `Timeline`, `Pagination`, `Toolbar`, `EmptyState`/`ErrorState`, `StatCard`, `ConfirmDialog`, plus missing primitives `Textarea`, `Checkbox`, `Tabs`, `FileDropzone`.

**Integration boundary:** contract into `openapi/fixture.yaml` → `pnpm gen:api` types → real hooks → MSW handlers **only under `VITE_USE_MSW`**. Production calls the real endpoint and shows the normal ProblemDetail error until the backend lands. No mock data in shipped code paths.

## 5. Backend dependencies

Ordered by blocking weight: **refunds domain** (entirely new: entity, state machine, payment reversal); **order status expansion** (`PLACED/SHIPPED/DELIVERED` → adds `PACKED`, and the portal needs `IN_TRANSIT`/`OUT_FOR_DELIVERY`/`DELAYED`/`DAMAGED`/`LOST`); **buyer order history**; **store profile** entity with unique `handle`; **product status** `ACTIVE/DRAFT`; **metrics aggregation** + a product-view tracking pipeline that doesn't exist at all; **payments** (refunds imply a provider — today checkout takes no payment); **ops identity/role** + parcel/scan/hub domain.

## 6. Decisions and questions

**Design inconsistencies I found:**
- Three different status vocabularies across buyer/seller/portal screens — needs one canonical model with a display mapping.
- Seller Products uses free-text categories (`Headphones`, `Earbuds`); main just moved to system categories with slugs. Direct conflict.
- Refund amounts don't match line totals (`112` vs `99` unit price) — shipping/tax proration rules undefined.
- "Visa ending 5567" appears throughout; there is no payment method stored anywhere.
- Dashboard charts "views" — nothing tracks impressions.
- `Replacement` outcome implies shipping a replacement order; that lifecycle is undefined.

**Four questions before I start:**

1. **Versioning.** The existing API is unversioned (`/products`, `/sellers/me/...`). Mixing `/api/v1/*` for new routes with unprefixed old ones is the worst outcome. My recommendation: backend serves everything under `/api/v1` and keeps current paths as aliases through one deprecation window; frontend flips `baseUrl` once. Agree, or keep new-only under `/api/v1`?

2. **Is Amezo Portal in scope now?** It's a third persona with its own auth, roles and an entire logistics domain (hubs, parcels, scans). I'd defer it to its own phase and ship buyer + seller first.

3. **Seller Dashboard.** Build it against the full metrics contract including views (blocked on tracking that doesn't exist), or cut to orders/revenue/top-products which are derivable from existing data today?

4. **Drawer vs full page.** Designs default to "Side drawer" with a toggle. I'd use drawers for the seller product/order forms, full page for buyer Order Detail. Confirm?

**Suggested phasing:** A) buyer orders + refund request · B) seller products/orders/refunds/store settings · C) dashboard · D) ops portal.

Awaiting your approval before I write any code.

`checkbox` · `radio-group` · `switch` · `label` · `tabs` · `separator` · `skeleton` · `tooltip` · `alert` · `pagination` · `sonner` · `chart` (pulls recharts) · `scroll-area` · `breadcrumb`

Components landed and the buyer flow is built. Pushed through `252afdd`.

## Two things about the components branch

**The CLI wrote them to `frontend/@/components/ui/`** — a literal `@` directory, not the resolved alias — so they were never on the import path. I moved all 14 into `src/components/ui/`.

Three fixes were needed to make them work here:
- They import `cn` from a third-party npm package of that name. This project has its own in `lib/utils`, which every existing component uses, so I pointed them there and dropped the package.
- `sonner` read the theme from `next-themes`. This is a Vite SPA with no such provider, so the toaster follows the OS and `next-themes` is dropped.
- The commit also carried a stock `radix-nova` `button.tsx`. Taking it would have restyled every button in the app — different radii, ring widths, a tinted destructive variant. I kept the in-use one.

**A real bug in three of them.** `checkbox`, `radio-group` and `switch` style their checked state with Tailwind's `data-checked:` variant, which compiles to `[data-checked]`. The Radix primitive in this project emits `data-state="checked"`. Net effect: a checked radio looked identical to an unchecked one. I verified it against the rendered DOM before touching anything, then changed only the variant selector — the classes themselves are untouched. Worth knowing if you regenerate them.

## Screens done: 2 of 9

**My Orders** — search, period filter, the four state tabs, order cards expanding into the delivery timeline, attached refund, line items and totals. The list returns a summary, so expanding is what fetches the order rather than sending everything for every row.

**Refund Request** — line selection, the 20-character detail floor, refund vs replacement, payout choice. Whether a refund can be raised is the server's `canRequestRefund`, not a date comparison done in the UI; an order that says no redirects instead of showing a form that would be refused.

One contract change: `RefundPayout` is now `ORIGINAL_PAYMENT | ALTERNATE_METHOD`. The design offers "the card you paid with" or "that card is closed, contact me" — not store credit, which this marketplace has no concept of. Handoff doc updated.

Date formatting is written out rather than handed to `Intl` — the designs are day-first with three-letter months, and no locale gives that (en-US reorders to "Sep 17", en-GB writes "Sept").

**284 tests pass** (11 new), lint unchanged, build clean.

## Remaining: 7 screens

Seller Products + Product Form, Seller Orders + Order Detail, Seller Refunds, Store Settings, Seller Dashboard — all seller-side, all needing the drawer/full-page pattern. The contract and hooks for every one of them are already in place, so it's UI work from here.

I'm stopping to let you see the two finished screens before I build the other seven the same way. Say go and I'll continue.

[LOG_ENTRY type=RESPONSE num=8 session=28c0ad96]
timestamp: 2026-09-26T07:04:47.463Z
model: claude-opus-5

All nine screens are done and pushed through `b633f58`.

## Built this round

**Seller Products** — search, status/category filters, sort, pagination, stock warnings, inline delete confirm. **Seller Orders** — search, status filter, sort, refund flag on rows. **Seller Refunds** — tabs opening on what needs a decision, approve/decline/receive/release. **Store Settings** — identity, about with the 600-char limit, vacation mode, live preview. **Seller Dashboard** — range tabs, four headline tiles with deltas, revenue and orders charts, top products, revenue by category, three queue widgets.

**Drawer + full page** works as you asked: rows open a drawer, the "Full page" button opens the same panel at its own route in a new tab. That's the *same component* in both — I extracted the existing 600-line product form into `ProductFormPanel` rather than writing a second copy.

The dashboard's queue widgets are the existing lists with a filter (`?status=PLACED`, `?stockBelow=10`, `?status=REQUESTED`), not new endpoints.

## Three bugs found along the way

**The shadcn components had a systemic styling bug.** They style state with Tailwind's bare `data-open:` / `data-active:` / `data-horizontal:` variants, which compile to attribute-presence selectors (`[data-open]`). Radix emits `data-state="open"` and `data-orientation="horizontal"` — so none of it matched. Dialogs and sheets had no transition, and the selected tab looked like every other tab. Eight components affected. I confirmed this against the compiled CSS rather than reasoning about it, and changed only the variant selectors. Same root cause as the checkbox/radio/switch fix earlier. **Worth knowing if you regenerate them.**

**Demo mode was unreachable.** `SellerAuthContext`'s boot check opts out via `DEMO_AUTH`, but its `api:unauthorized` listener didn't — so the 401 that `/sessions/current` returns for a visitor signed the demo seller straight back out. Pre-existing on main; found because I couldn't screenshot the portal.

**A query-key namespace bug of my own** — the v1 lists were keyed separately from the existing hooks, so deleting a product left it on screen. The v1 keys now nest under `['seller','products']` and `['seller','orders']`.

## Contract changes since the handoff doc

`PATCH /api/v1/sellers/me/orders/{id}` takes `handoverMethod`/`hub`; `trackingNumber` is **not** writable, because the design says it's issued by the platform on handover. `RefundPayout` became `ORIGINAL_PAYMENT | ALTERNATE_METHOD`. The unversioned endpoints now share the widened `OrderStatus` instead of declaring their own. All reflected in `docs/backend-handoff.md`.

## Left out, deliberately

The order panel's compose box has a **"Refunded" stage** in the design — a seller refunding without the buyer raising anything. The refund resource is buyer-raised today, so building it would mean two paths to the same state. Flagged in the handoff with what it'd need.

## Verification

308 unit tests (39 new), 5 Playwright specs, lint at the pre-existing baseline of 10, build clean. One e2e spec was stale — it clicked through hardcoded Gulf cities in a header control main has since replaced with a real country picker; updated.

Appended **last** on purpose: the seller queue is served in store order and `SellerRefunds.test.tsx` reaches for rows by position, so nothing existing moved. Its buyer name/email/line match the demo order it belongs to.

  - `POST /api/v1/refund-requests` now calls `addRefundRequest(created)` alongside the existing `attachRefundToOrder`. This is required for the order-side derivation to be honest, and it incidentally fixes a real 404: `GET /api/v1/refund-requests/:id` for a request the buyer had just created missed the store entirely and fell through to the buyer-order seed array.
  
  **`src/test/msw/fixtures/sellerOrders.test.ts` (new, 6 tests)** — the allowlist confined me to `src/test/msw/**`, so the cover lives beside the fixture rather than in `SellerOrderPanel.test.tsx`. Tests go over HTTP through MSW exactly as the app's hooks do:

The clean fix is to derive the buyer side from the refund store the way I did for the seller side — but that first needs the duplicated seed reconciled: `buyerOrders.refundRequests[0]` is id `ref-1111` while `refunds.ts` has id `ref-3` carrying the *same* reference `ref_90ce34aa`, and `MyOrders.test.tsx` asserts against the buyer-side copy. Worth collapsing the two fixtures one day, but that's a rewrite, not a bug fix.

Pushed. Here's the design-gap backlog for you to prioritise.

**The one I'd look at first is #2**, because it isn't really a styling gap. The store page never calls `GET /api/v1/stores/{handle}` at all — `useStoreProducts.ts:22` fetches `GET /products?size=100` and filters by `brandName` in the browser, and the route is `/stores/:brand` rather than by handle. So the cover, logo, about text and policies have nowhere to come from, and the page does an N-product scan to render one store. Wiring the endpoint we already defined fixes the design gap and deletes the scan in the same change.

**Cheap wins** (all S, no new API): Recent-orders widget (#6 — the hook is already imported on that page), the category donut (#7 — recharts is already a dependency), numbered pagination (#9 — `src/components/ui/pagination.tsx` exists and nothing imports it), and dashboard row actions (#14).

**Blocked on API surface that doesn't exist**: store-image uploads (#3), tab counts and bucket values (#8), per-product vs-prev delta (#10), edit-review (#11), the Refunded compose stage (#12), image reorder (#13), product specs and wishlist (#16).

Two of the original review claims **did not survive checking**, and I've recorded them as such rather than quietly dropping them:
- Buyer-facing reviews *are* implemented — `ProductDetail.tsx:117` has the tab, average, paged list and eligibility-gated form. Only the *inline* block inside an expanded order is missing.
- The seller order drawer is *meant* to be editable; ours matches the design closely. The read-only-drawer gap applies to Products only.

I also closed a gap I'd opened earlier in this pass: I'd made `status` writable on `PATCH /products/{productRef}` without `SellerProductDetail` returning it, so the edit form couldn't show a listing's state, and `CreateProductRequest` had no `status` — the only route to DRAFT was publish-then-unpublish. Both are in the contract now, so #4 is UI-only work.

Two things to flag:

- **The ProductFormPanel agent never reported back** — it's been running ~15 minutes. Its changes are in the tree, typecheck clean and passing (97 tests across the seller areas), and they're committed. I haven't seen its account of what it did or what it chose not to fix, so that one file's fixes are verified by tests but not by its own report.
- The four follow-ups from my last message (loading-looks-like-empty widgets, stale buyer-order refund summaries, history spam per keystroke, unmounted `<Toaster>`) are still open and not in this document — they're bugs, not design gaps.

  ## Price-zero rule and reasoning
  
  Empty/whitespace/NaN → missing for both fields. Stock accepts a genuine `0` (and requires a non-negative integer) because zero stock is how a seller stops selling a variant — the Variants panel says exactly that. Price must be **strictly greater than 0**: a $0.00 listing is not a price a seller means (a giveaway is a promotion, not a zero-priced offer), and `0` is precisely the value the `Number('')` bug produced — accepting a typed `0` would leave the same mispriced live listing one stray keystroke away, indistinguishable from the bug.   - `SellerProductDetail`/`SellerVariant` carry no `updatedAt` or version field, so identity is the id alone. Freshness after a save comes from the mutation response instead. That is the inherent trade-off of the fix (the Images section, which renders straight from props, still updates live).
  - If a save is in flight and the seller keeps typing, the response re-seeds over that typing. Narrow race, unchanged from before.
  - The inline validation message clears on the next save attempt, not on keystroke, matching the surrounding error handling; a stale mutation error is not `reset()` — the field message just takes precedence in the shared slot.
  - I left `min="0"` on the price inputs rather than tightening it to `"0.01"`: the rule is enforced in JS with the house inline message, and a native constraint would produce a browser bubble instead.
  - The `required` attributes on the add-product and new-variant price/stock inputs remain; the JS checks are now the real guard (whitespace satisfies `required`, and nothing enforces it on a programmatic submit).

Neither commit is broken — both typecheck and pass their suites, which is why my pre-commit full run was green. The real defect is that **neither commit message describes the ProductFormPanel work**; it rode along in both. I've recorded exactly that in the log commit rather than leaving it undocumented.

I did **not** rewrite the two pushed commits to fix their messages. That's a force-push on a branch you may have pulled, and the branch will likely be squash-merged anyway. Say the word if you'd rather I re-split them properly — it's a soft reset and two clean commits.

On the fix itself, the agent made a judgement call worth your attention: **price must be strictly greater than zero**, stock accepts a genuine `0`. Its reasoning is that zero stock is how a seller stops selling a variant, whereas `0` is precisely the value the `Number('')` bug produced — so accepting a typed `0` would leave the same mispriced live listing one keystroke away and indistinguishable from the bug. That rules out $0.00 listings entirely. If you ever want free items, that rule needs revisiting.

Two trade-offs it accepted, which I'd want you to know about:
- Because `SellerProductDetail`/`SellerVariant` carry no `updatedAt` or version field, identity is the id alone. So a refetch bringing newer server values for a field the seller *isn't* editing no longer updates that input until remount. That's inherent to the fix, not an oversight.

  Fixed the search-box history bug on the three seller pages. Only the six allowlisted files were touched; no git state changes, no `pnpm gen:api`, no full test-suite run.
  
  ## The shape of the fix (identical in all three pages)
  
  `patch` gained an optional second parameter and passes it through, everything else about it (the `'all'` handling, the `if (!('page' in next)) params.delete('page')` reset rule) is byte-identical:
  
  ```tsx
  // `replace` swaps the current history entry instead of pushing a new one.
  function patch(next: Record<string, string | undefined>, replace = false) {
    ...unchanged...
    setSearchParams(params, { replace })
  }
  
  // A filter, a sort or a page is a navigation the seller may want to undo
  // with Back; a keystroke is not. The first character pushes the one entry that
  // Back escapes the search by, and every character after it replaces that entry
  // - typing "laptop" used to leave six entries to press Back through.
  function patchTerm(value: string) {
    setTerm(value)
    patch({ q: value }, Boolean(q))
  }
  ```
  
  and the search input's `onChange` became `onChange={(e) => patchTerm(e.target.value)}` — no other markup, class, spacing or sizing change anywhere (verified in the diff: the inputs and every wrapper are untouched apart from that one attribute).
  
  Note the `Boolean(q)`: the *first* keystroke of a search still pushes, every keystroke after it replaces. That is what makes "one typed search = exactly one history entry, and one Back returns you to the page as it was before you started typing" true. Replacing unconditionally would have destroyed the pre-typing entry and made Back leave the page entirely. Filters, sorts, tabs, Previous/Next and "Clear filters" all still push (untouched code paths).
  
  Files:

[LOG_ENTRY type=RESPONSE num=23 session=28c0ad96]
timestamp: 2026-09-26T09:53:51.100Z
model: claude-opus-5

Yes, we are — react-router v8, and `setSearchParams` *is* react-router. The fix didn't add anything outside it.

```
src/pages/seller/SellerProducts.tsx:3
import { Link, useSearchParams } from 'react-router'
```

`useSearchParams` returns `[searchParams, setSearchParams]`, and `setSearchParams` takes a second argument — the same `NavigateOptions` object `navigate()` takes. `{ replace }` is react-router's own option, not something hand-rolled:

```
src/pages/seller/SellerProducts.tsx:105
setSearchParams(params, { replace })
```

Under the hood it picks `history.pushState` vs `history.replaceState`. Push is the default.

**So why was there a bug at all?** Because react-router can't make this call for you. It has no way to know whether a given URL change is *a navigation* (something you'd expect Back to undo) or *an edit to the view you're already on*. Both are "the URL changed". So it defaults to push, which is right for almost every call — and the search box was the one place that default was wrong, because `onChange` fires per keystroke. Six characters, six `pushState` calls, six Back presses.

The fix is just taking the option react-router already offers, at the one call site that needs it. Every other call still pushes, deliberately:

| Call site | Behaviour | Why |
|---|---|---|
| Search box (`patchTerm`) | replace after the 1st char | keystrokes aren't navigations |
| Status/category/sort selects, tabs | push | you may want Back to undo a filter |
| Previous / Next | push | paging is navigation |
| "Clear filters" | push | a deliberate action worth undoing |

The `Boolean(q)` is the part worth a second look:

```
src/pages/seller/SellerProducts.tsx:112
function patchTerm(value: string) {
  setTerm(value)
  patch({ q: value }, Boolean(q))
}
```

`q` is the URL's value *before* this keystroke. On the first character it's `''` → falsy → **push**, which creates the single entry that Back uses to escape the search. Every character after that sees a non-empty `q` → **replace**, folding into that one entry. If it replaced unconditionally you'd get the opposite bug: the first keystroke would overwrite the entry you arrived on, and Back would throw you off the page entirely rather than back to the unfiltered list.

One inconsistency this surfaced that I left alone: `src/features/search/hooks/useSearchFilters.ts:48` still calls `setSearchParams(toParams(next))` with no options. That's the buyer `/search` page, and it's fine — its filters come from the sidebar and sort dropdown, not a per-keystroke input (the search *term* there is submitted from the header, not typed into the URL). So it has no keystroke problem to fix. Worth knowing it's a different code path from the four list pages, though, if you ever add a live-filter input there.

There's a test for it: PATCH the handle to `aurora-sound` and the public storefront moves to the new handle, `aurora-audio` 404s, and the listings come with it (matching is by store **id**, never by handle, so a rename can't orphan a catalogue).

Everything is currently green: **51 files, 405 tests**, `tsc` and lint clean. Status:

**Landed and verified:**
- The storefront reads `GET /api/v1/stores/{handle}` and `.../products` — the 100-product scan is gone from the page
- MSW fixtures derive the public store from the seller's own profile rather than duplicating it. There's a test that renames the handle in Store Settings and confirms the public storefront moves with it, listings intact — matching is by store **id**, never handle, so a rename can't orphan a catalogue
- Routing moved to `/stores/:handle`, with `/stores/Aurora%20Audio` still resolving and redirecting

**Two things I changed after agent reports, both worth flagging:**

1. **`POST .../messages` now requires a buyer session.** Agent A built it open and asked. The dialog promises "will reply to the address on your account" — which presumes one — and an endpoint that emails sellers on behalf of anonymous callers is a spam relay. I added the 401 to the contract, gated the handler, and made the Message button a sign-in link when signed out, same as Follow. That broke agent A's 7 messaging tests, which were written for the open behaviour; I updated them and added one asserting the 401.

2. **`ProductDetail` gained `store`.** Agent A spotted that the product detail payload had only `brandName`, so the product page had no handle to link with — agent B would have been stuck. Added to the contract before it hit that.

**One limitation worth knowing:** the legacy-URL redirect still does a 100-product scan to turn a display name into a handle. It's bounded to deprecated URLs only — the storefront itself never scans — but a legacy link for a store whose listings fall outside the first 100 products won't resolve. Reading the handle off a listing is right (slugifying the name would guess at a server-owned key and produce dead links); fixing the bound properly would mean a lookup endpoint for a URL shape we're deprecating, which I don't think is worth it.

I'll commit the whole feature as one change once agent B reports.

**Judgement calls you may want to overrule:**

1. **Messages require a buyer session.** Agent A built it open and asked. I closed it — the dialog promises a reply "to the address on your account", and an endpoint emailing sellers for anonymous callers is a spam relay. That broke its 7 messaging tests, which I updated.
2. **`medianResponseMinutes`, not "Under 2h"** — a display string in the API means a wording change needs a backend release.
3. **In-store search on the page, not in the header.** The one deliberate deviation from the design. That mock's header is simplified (no categories row, delivery picker or account); retrofitting a store-scoped mode into the shared buyer header risks every route for one page. Easy to move if you disagree.

**One limitation worth knowing:** legacy `/stores/Aurora%20Audio` links resolve via a 100-product scan — the same reach the old storefront had, so nothing that worked before breaks, but a store whose listings fall outside that page won't resolve. Reading the handle off a listing is right; slugifying the name would guess a server-owned key and produce dead links. I've noted a `GET /api/v1/stores?name=` resolver in the handoff doc as the exact fix, only needed while the old URLs are supported.

Remaining backlog is `docs/design-gaps.md` — #1 (read-only product drawer) and #3 (Store Settings branding) are the next substantial ones; #6, #7, #9 and #14 are all small.

Added a validated `?size=` param (`5/10/20/50`, default 10 — the old fixed `PAGE_SIZE`). Filter/size changes still drop `?page=`; the typed-search push-then-replace behaviour is unchanged.
  - *Coupling note:* this page now depends on `PaginationBar`/`range.onSizeChange` and the `Rows per page` aria-label from that in-flight file. `tsc -b` is currently clean; if that agent renames those, this page and two of its tests move with it.

  ## The two transition questions
  
  1. **`REQUESTED → REPLACEMENT_SENT` is not legal.** `LEGAL` in `src/test/msw/fixtures/refunds.ts` has `REQUESTED: ['APPROVED','DECLINED','CANCELLED']`; `REPLACEMENT_SENT` is only reachable from `APPROVED` or `RETURN_RECEIVED`, and `handlers.ts:508` 409s anything else. So "Send replacement" writes the two steps the server accepts — `APPROVED`, then `REPLACEMENT_SENT` — behind one button, with a line in the UI saying it is recorded that way. If the second write fails, the request sits at `APPROVED`, where "Mark replacement sent" offers it again; the error surfaces in the panel. Covered by a test.
  2. **"Undo approval" is blocked — left out.** `APPROVED → [AWAITING_RETURN, RETURN_RECEIVED, REPLACEMENT_SENT]`, `AWAITING_RETURN → [RETURN_RECEIVED]`. Nothing walks an approved request back to `REQUESTED`, and `CANCELLED` is only reachable from `REQUESTED` (and is the buyer's move). The button is absent, with a comment saying why, and a test asserts no Undo control exists.
  
  ## Also found
  
  - **`UpdateRefundRequest` carries no `resolution`**, so a seller choosing an outcome other than the buyer's cannot record that choice. Consequence: approving an amount against a `REPLACEMENT` request used to leave "Mark replacement sent" as the only next move, with `REFUNDED` unreachable. `isReplacementPath()` now treats a recorded `approvedAmount` as the money path, which removes that dead end. A `resolution` field on the update contract would be the real fix.
  - The PATCH handler **ignores `note`** — it is neither stored nor echoed, so the note the seller writes is never shown to anyone. We still send it. Not fixed: `src/test/msw/**` is off-limits and the real backend may well persist it.

  - `docs/design-gaps.md` not updated (outside the allowlist).
  - The design's orange-tinted decision card, pill-shaped segments and drawer chrome: kept the existing shadcn visuals, per "preserve the existing visual design".
  - The design's single "Mark returned and release $X": kept as the two steps the map allows (`RETURN_RECEIVED`, then `REFUNDED`) — collapsing them would claim money moved when it had not.
  - The design's settled-state card: the status badge, stepper and the new History already say the same thing.
  - Labels `Refund amount` and `Approve and request return` kept (design says "Amount to refund" / "Approve and send return label") so existing assertions keep testing behaviour rather than being rewritten around a rename.
  
  ## Tests
  New file **`src/features/refunds/components/RefundDecisionPanel.test.tsx`** (8): one decision at a time (amount and decline reason are never both on screen); panel opens on the buyer's resolution; **Full $X** fills the amount and flips the note; decline stays disabled until a sentence is written, then declines; payout shown; alternate payout named (via a `server.use` detail override); replacement goes through the approval the server insists on; approved state offers only the legal move and has no Undo.
  
  **`src/pages/seller/SellerRefunds.test.tsx`** (+2): numbered pages with the range label, Prev/Next disabled at the ends (a local `server.use` serves 8 synthetic requests — the seeded queue is one page however it is sliced); per-page selector resizes and resets to the first page.
  
  **One existing assertion changed deliberately:** `findAllByText('Approved')` in "approves a request from the drawer" went `2 → 3`, because the panel now also logs "Approved" in History; the comment above it says so. Row ordering is untouched, so the positional row lookups still hold.

With no `range` the bar centres itself, which is where the search grid's pager has always sat. Built from `Pagination`/`PaginationContent`/`PaginationItem`/`PaginationEllipsis`.
  - **`PaginationButton`** — why I had to touch the shared file: stock `PaginationLink` (and therefore `PaginationPrevious`/`PaginationNext`) renders an `<a>`. These lists page by rewriting a `?page=` search param, not by following an href, and an anchor cannot be `disabled`, which Prev and Next are at the ends of the range. `PaginationButton` is the same `Button` styling with button semantics, `aria-current="page"` and the design's filled current page (`variant="default"`, i.e. primary). Stock exports are untouched, so nothing that uses them changes.
  - **Ellipsis window**: seven slots once it starts eliding — `1 2 3 4 5 … 20`, `1 … 9 10 11 … 20`, `1 … 16 17 18 19 20` — so the row never changes width and fifty pages never render fifty buttons.
  
  ## Per page
  
  Each page declares `PAGE_SIZES = [5, 10, 20, 50]`, `DEFAULT_SIZE = 10` and a `sizeParam()` helper beside its existing `pageParam()` (matching the file-local convention already there — the constant can't be shared from `pagination.tsx` without adding a 9th lint warning). Junk (`?size=abc`) and anything off the offered set (`?size=10000`) fall back to the default. Size changes go through the existing `patch()`, which drops `?page=` for any non-page key, so per-page resets to page 0 and pushes history like a filter; `?size=10` is omitted from the URL.
  
  The bar renders whenever there are rows, not only past page one — otherwise at 50-a-page there's one page and no way back to 5. That's the designs' `hasRows` condition.
  
  ## Per file
  - **`SellerProducts.tsx`** / **`SellerOrders.tsx`** / **`MyOrders.tsx`** — `PAGE_SIZE` → `PAGE_SIZES`/`DEFAULT_SIZE` + `sizeParam`, `size` into the filters memo and its deps, Previous/Page-N-of-M/Next block → `PaginationBar`. `MyOrders` passes `unit: 'orders'` ("Showing 1–5 of 8 orders", as its design reads); the seller designs have no unit. Existing spacing preserved (`pt-6` on My Orders). `pageParam`, the controlled search box, `patch(next, replace)` push/replace semantics and the `shownPage` clamp are untouched.
  - **`useSearchFilters.ts`** — `page: page ? Number(page) : 0` gave `NaN` for `?page=abc`; now the same defensive `pageParam` the other three use.
  - **`SearchResults.tsx`** — numbered pager only.
  - **`ResultsHeader.tsx`** — **unchanged**, see below.
  
  ## SearchResults did need a different approach
  Its list size is fixed in `src/features/search/schema/types.ts` (`PAGE_SIZE = 16`, and `SearchFilters` has no `size` field), and `src/features/search/api/useSearchProducts.ts` hardcodes `size: PAGE_SIZE` into the request. **Both are outside the allowlist**, so a Per page select there would have been a control that changes nothing — I left it out. Its range label already exists in `ResultsHeader` ("1 - 8 over 42 results for …"), so `SearchResults` renders `PaginationBar` without `range` — numbered pager only, still centred, no duplicate label. `ResultsHeader` is only in the allowlist because its `PAGE_SIZE` import is what would have become a prop; with size fixed it needed no change. **To finish gap 9 there, add `size` to `SearchFilters`/`DEFAULT_FILTERS` and have `useSearchProducts` send `filters.size`; `useSearchFilters` and `ResultsHeader` would then need ~5 lines each.**
  
  ## Tests added (12)
  - `SellerProducts.test.tsx` (4): numbered paging + range label on a short last page (`Showing 6–8 of 8`) + `aria-current`; long-range elision (8 pages → 1 2 3 4 5 … 8, no "6"); page size change resets to `?size=20` with no `?page=`; junk and oversized `?size=` both ask the server for `10`.
  - `SellerOrders.test.tsx` (3) and `MyOrders.test.tsx` (3): the same three, minus elision. My Orders' seed has only two orders, so its pagination tests install a `server.use` override that pages eight copies of the seeded order — no fixture files touched.
  - `SearchResults.test.tsx` (2): numbered paging (a `server.use` override splits the six seeded products three a page, since the page's own size is fixed) and `?page=abc` reading as page one.

  pnpm exec oxlint <the 6 paths>         → clean, no output

Three of four Wave 1 agents are done; two are resumed on follow-ups I unblocked.

**Gap 5 (refunds) closed** — three-way segmented control, Full-$X shortcut, decline requiring an explanation, `payout` surfaced. Two findings I acted on:

- **`UpdateRefundRequest` had no `resolution`**, so a seller settling a replacement request with money couldn't record it — the agent worked around it by inferring the outcome from whether an amount happened to be set. I've added the field and sent it back to drop the inference.
- **`note` was accepted by the contract and stored nowhere.** A seller writes a note believing the buyer sees it, and nobody ever did. Rather than bolt a `sellerNote` field on, I made the timeline a real resource: `RefundEvent { status, at, note }` with `RefundRequestDetail.events`, and the handler appends one per transition. History becomes a read of that instead of a reconstruction from scattered timestamps — which is the only way a note can attach to the step it was written for.

The agent also checked two transitions rather than assuming: `REQUESTED → REPLACEMENT_SENT` is **not** legal, so "Send replacement" writes the two steps the server accepts behind one button; and nothing walks an approved request back, so it left **Undo approval out** with a test asserting no such control exists. Both correct — I confirmed against the documented state machine.

**Gap 9 (pagination) closed** — one `PaginationBar` across all five lists. It had to add `PaginationButton` because stock `PaginationLink` renders an `<a>`, and these lists page by rewriting `?page=`; an anchor can't be `disabled`, which Prev/Next are at the ends. Stock exports untouched.

It was honest about a limit: **SearchResults has no per-page selector**, because its size is hardcoded in `search/schema/types.ts` and `useSearchProducts.ts`, both outside its allowlist — so the control would have changed nothing. It left it out rather than shipping a dead select, and named the ~10 lines needed to finish it.

**Gap 4 + 13 closed** — status toggle in both modes, title counter, cover badge, duplicate variant, summary strip, and image reorder now round-tripping through the endpoint I added.

My own YAML splice went wrong twice this round — `events` landed inside `lines:`, producing a duplicate mapping key. `gen:api` caught it; I read the file back and repaired it rather than trusting the earlier "ok".

Dashboard agent (gaps 6, 7, 10, 14, 15) is still running.

  ## The four defects
  
  **1. Ragged grids / orphan panels — fixed.**
  Both bands now use `grid gap-4 lg:grid-cols-2` instead of `repeat(auto-fit, minmax(…))`. Four panels into two columns is 2×2 with no remainder, at every width, whatever order the seller drags them into — auto-fit could only ever give 1, 2, 3 or 4 columns, and 3 is what it chose at 1440. Charts now read Revenue | Orders / Top products | Revenue by category; widgets Waiting to ship | Low stock / Recent orders | Refunds to review. This also restores the original chart-panel width (`lg:grid-cols-2` is exactly the class the two old chart grids used). You were right that my "column counts unchanged" claim was wrong — I reasoned about it from the old 2-and-3 panel grids and never re-checked once each band held four.
  
  **2. "Refunds to review" wrapping to three lines — fixed.**
  The panel header is now `flex-wrap` and the `<h2>` is `min-w-0 truncate`. Two independent guarantees: the title can never break mid-phrase (it ellipsises at worst), and when the grip + title + note + link + both buttons genuinely don't fit, the action group drops to a second line as a block. At 1440 it all sits on one line; at 1024 (the tight case) the title is on line 1 and `3 open · $687.97  Refunds  ‹ ›` on line 2, right-aligned — which is what the 1024 screenshot confirms.
  
  **3. Top products table overflowing — fixed.**
  The table is now `table-fixed` with the design's column widths (`#` 26px, Product auto, Revenue 92px, Share 56px). With `auto` layout the long product title sized its own column and pushed Revenue and Share past the panel edge, where `Table`'s `overflow-x-auto` wrapper hid them. Fixed layout gives the title cell a bounded width so its `truncate` finally bites. At 1440 all four titles fit in full with both numeric columns visible; at 1024 titles ellipsise ("14" Ultrabook La…") and the numbers stay put. Also truncated the "N units · $X each" line for the same reason.
  
  **4. Donut dead space — fixed.**
  Two causes. The legend's `basis-[200px]` couldn't fit beside the 168px ring in a 3-up panel, so it wrapped *under* the donut and doubled the panel's height; it is now `basis-[150px]` and sits beside the ring at every width. And `Panel` is now a `flex flex-col` whose body is `flex-1`, with the donut's root `flex-1 … items-center`, so a body shorter than the grid row centres itself instead of leaving all the slack at the bottom. Panels in a row are still the same height, so the grid stays even.

Four agents are running on gaps 3, 11, 10's delta column and SearchResults per-page. While they work, I've added the contract for gap 8.

**One thing I've deliberately not built, because it's an architecture decision rather than a coding one.**

Gap 12 wants a **"Refunded" stage in the seller's order composer**, with an amount and a reason. Implementing it means adding `REFUNDED` to `OrderStatus` — and that would give this app **two sources of truth for the same fact**. Refunds are already modelled properly: a `refund_request` with its own state machine, its own approval and return steps, its own money field, and a whole seller queue built on it. An order status that also says "refunded" would drift from it the first time someone settles a refund through the queue instead of the composer, and nothing would reconcile the two.

My read is that the design's Order Detail composer predates the Refunds screen and the two overlap. The honest options:

1. **Drop the Refunded stage** from the composer and let the Refunds queue own refunds — the composer links to it instead.
2. **Add `REFUNDED` to `OrderStatus`** as a *derived* value the server computes from the refund request, never written by the composer.
3. **Build it as designed** and accept two writable sources.

I'd take (1), and (2) if you want the word to appear on the order row. I don't recommend (3). Tell me which and I'll do it — I didn't want to bake a duplicate state machine into the contract on my own judgement.

What I *did* add for gap 12 is uncontroversial: `UpdateSellerOrder.occurredAt`, so a seller recording yesterday's handover today can say when it actually happened rather than having it stamped now.

For gap 8 I added three `facets` endpoints — one each for seller orders, the refund queue and buyer orders. The design's tabs show counts for *every* bucket at once, so they can't be a field on the filtered list: filtering to PLACED would zero every other tab. Each facet's `key` is whatever the matching list endpoint accepts, so a tab becomes a filter with no translation table.

Pushed (`493d835`) — verified green at **464 tests / 54 files**, lint at 8, before staging. Only my files were dirty; the four agents hadn't written yet.

That commit is the groundwork the running agents need: the store-image upload pair and review editing now work end to end rather than existing only in the contract, plus the three `facets` endpoints for gap 8.

Two details worth pulling out, both cases where the obvious implementation would have been wrong:

- **The store-image confirm names the slot twice** — once when reserving, once when confirming — and refuses a mismatch. Without it you can reserve a cover and confirm it as a logo, putting a 1600×400 banner into an 88px circle. Nothing touches the profile until confirm, so an abandoned upload leaves it untouched rather than half-applied.
- **Facets are a separate resource, not a field on the list.** The tabs show a count for every bucket simultaneously, so embedding them in a filtered response would zero every tab except the one you're looking at.

Still open for your call, from my last message: **gap 12's Refunded stage**. I've committed everything else it needs (`occurredAt` for backdating) but deliberately not `REFUNDED` on `OrderStatus`, because refunds already have a full state machine in `refund_request` and a writable order status saying the same thing is a second source of truth nothing reconciles. My preference is to drop the composer stage and let the Refunds queue own it; second choice is a server-derived `REFUNDED` that the composer can't write.

The four agents are on gaps 3 (branding), 11 (inline reviews), 10's vs-prev column, and the SearchResults page size. I'll verify each — including screenshotting the dashboard column, since that's where a green suite lied to us last time — and commit as they land.

  Design gap 9 (buyer search Per page selector) is done. `tsc -b`, the three target test files and `oxlint` are all clean, repo lint still at 8 warnings.
  
  ## Changes per file
  
  **`src/features/search/schema/types.ts`**
  - Replaced `PAGE_SIZE = 16` with `PAGE_SIZES = [5, 10, 20, 50] as const` and `DEFAULT_SIZE = 10`, the same file-local pair the other four lists declare.
  - `SearchFilters` gains `size: number`; `DEFAULT_FILTERS.size = DEFAULT_SIZE`.
  
  **`src/features/search/hooks/useSearchFilters.ts`**
  - Added `sizeParam()` next to `pageParam()`, same defensive shape: `Number(raw)`, then `PAGE_SIZES.some(...) ? parsed : DEFAULT_FILTERS.size`. Junk, `NaN`, negatives and anything off the offered set (e.g. `?size=10000`) all collapse to 10, so nothing unclamped reaches the request.
  - `parseFilters` reads `?size=`; `toParams` writes it only when it differs from the default, mirroring the existing `sort` line — so the default size keeps the URL clean.
  - No new reset mechanism: `update()` already zeroes `page` unless `resetPage: false`, so the page calls `update({ size })` and the existing filter-reset path does the work.
  
  **`src/features/search/api/useSearchProducts.ts`** — sends `size: filters.size`; dropped the `PAGE_SIZE` import.
  
  **`src/features/search/components/ResultsHeader.tsx`** — dropped the `start`/`end` arithmetic and with it the now-unused `page` and `resultCount` props. It prints `42 results for "q"` / `42 products` plus the sort select; markup, classes and wording are otherwise untouched.
  
  **`src/pages/SearchResults.tsx`** — passes `range` (`totalElements`, `pageSize: filters.size`, `sizes: PAGE_SIZES`, `unit: 'products'`, `onSizeChange: (size) => update({ size })`) to the existing `PaginationBar`, and renders it whenever there are products rather than only when `totalPages > 1` — matching the other four ("Per page is how you get back from 50 to 5, and at 50 there is often only one page"), and keeping a count on screen for single-page results now that the header no longer prints one.
  
  ## Which range label survived, and why
  **The pager's.** `PaginationBar` renders the range label and the Per page select from the same `range` object — there is no way to get the select without the label, and `pagination.tsx` is off-limits. So the header's range had to go. It was also the wrong one to keep on the merits: it computed `page * PAGE_SIZE + 1` from a constant it no longer owns, and it did not clamp, which is exactly the `?page=99` → "1585 - 1584 over 42" bug. `PaginationBar` clamps `current` before counting. The header keeps the total and the query echo, which the pager does not show and which must still appear on the zero-results screen where no pager renders. So: one range label, and the pre-existing out-of-range bug fell out with it (guarded by a test).
  
  ## Default size: 10, not 16
  Nothing asserted on 16 — `grep` found `PAGE_SIZE` only in the two search files I edited (`StoreFront.tsx` has its own unrelated `PAGE_SIZE = 20`), and the MSW `/products` handler's `?? 16` fallback is never reached because the client always sends a size. So the move cost no existing expectation. The deciding argument is that 16 is not in the offered set: a Radix `Select` with `value="16"` and items 5/10/20/50 renders an empty trigger, so keeping 16 would have meant either a broken control or a fifth, search-only size set — against the stated point of the shared bar ("so the five pagers stay one control rather than five"). What moved: the first page now shows 10 tiles instead of 16. Two existing tests changed their entry URL to `/search?size=5` (see below); no assertion about the design changed.

  - `resizes the request and goes back to the first page when Per page changes` — from `?size=5&page=1`, picks 20 in the Per page select, asserts the last request was `{ page: '0', size: '20' }` and that page one's first product is back.
  - `ignores a page size that is not a number` (`?size=abc`) — every request asks `size=10`, and the trigger reads 10.
  - `ignores a page size that is not one of the offered ones` (`?size=10000`) — same.
  - `does not print a count past the end for a hand-typed page number` (`?size=5&page=99`).
  - `counts a partial last page the way it fills it` — `?size=5&page=1` shows exactly one `article` and `Showing 6–6 of 6 products`.

Added an `askedParams()` helper using the `return undefined` fall-through idiom from `MyOrders.test.tsx`.
  
  I checked the new tests are not tautological: with `size` hard-coded back in the request, 3 tests fail; with a non-clamping `sizeParam`, the `?size=10000` test fails.

  Added the fifth column (`<TableHead className="h-8 w-[74px] pr-0 pl-2 text-right …">vs prev</TableHead>` and a matching cell) plus a local `VsPrevious` component. Direction is an arrow plus a word plus a colour, never colour alone: `ArrowUp`/`ArrowDown`/`Minus` at `size-2.5 strokeWidth={3.4}`, matching the design's 10px/3.4 arrows and StatTile's hexes (`#1f7a45` / `#b42318` / muted). The stale doc comment explaining why the column was omitted is replaced.
  
  **`/home/user/amezo/frontend/src/pages/seller/SellerDashboard.test.tsx`**
  One new test plus its fixture; `server.use` override inside the test file only. Nothing under `src/test/msw/**`, `openapi/fixture.yaml` or any other page was touched, and no git command was run.
  
  ## What the column prints, and why for a null previous
  
  - **previous non-zero →** `+50.0%` / `-25.0%`, arrow, green/red.
  - **`previousRevenue` null (or absent) →** an em-dash `—`, muted, no arrow, with `<span className="sr-only">No prior data</span>` carrying StatTile's exact wording. The reasoning: null means the product did not sell in the previous window, so no figure was ever measured for it. `+100%` is arithmetic against a number we do not have, and `New` is the word StatTile reserves for a **measured** zero — using it here would report an absence of data as an observed fact. A dash says "nothing to compare", which is what is true, and the design's own `renderVals()` does the same (`pct()` returns `'—'` when there is no previous). The words rather than the dash go to a screen reader because 74px has no room for them but a screen reader has no width limit.
  - **previous zero but non-null →** `New` with an up arrow, green — identical to StatTile. No division by zero, no `Infinity%`.
  - **genuinely unchanged (|change| < 0.05%) →** `Flat`, muted, with the `Minus` icon StatTile uses.
  
  Two bounds live in the helper so both surfaces share them: percentages drop the decimal once |change| ≥ 100 (`+248%`, not `+248.4%` — a tenth of a percent is noise on a tripling, and it keeps the figure inside 74px), and a rise past 999.5% prints `>999%`. A fall cannot pass −100%, so only the upper end needs a bound, and it is stated as the bound it passed rather than rounded to a figure we did not measure. This was not guesswork: I measured in Chromium that `+1234%` renders 72px and `+12388%` renders 81px in a 74px cell (`scrollWidth > clientWidth`), i.e. it would have spilled into the Share column. Capped, the widest label measures 63px inside a 66px content box.

  - `src/features/reviews/components/ReviewForm.tsx` — star fieldset replaced by `RatingStars` with the exact same classes; renders identically (its 8 existing tests still pass unchanged).
  - `src/features/orders/components/OrderCardDetail.tsx` — local `SectionLabel` swapped for the shared one; `<OrderReviews lines={order.lines} />` rendered after Items/totals, gated on `order.status === 'DELIVERED'` (matches the design's `canShowReviews`). Nothing else in the file moved.
  - `src/features/orders/components/OrderCard.tsx` — the `?review=1` pill (see below).
  
  ## How I tell a reviewed line from an unreviewed one
  
  `BuyerOrderLine` carries `id, productRef, productTitle, variantLabel, thumbnailUrl, quantity, unitPrice, lineTotal, refundRequestId, refundStatus` — **nothing review-shaped**. I did not add or fake a field.
  
  What it does give me is `productRef`, which is the key `GET /products/{productRef}/reviews/eligibility` takes, and that response carries `existingReview` (a full `Review`: id, rating, body, createdAt). So the published state is built from the eligibility answer, and `existingReview.id` is the `reviewId` the PATCH needs — there is no fabricated id anywhere. **Yes, this needs an extra request**: one per distinct product in an expanded order. The contract has no batched eligibility endpoint and no per-line review field, so a request per product is the honest minimum.
  
  Two consequences I handled rather than papered over:
  - Eligibility is **product-scoped, not line-scoped**, so two lines of the same product get one card, keyed by `productRef` (a second review of the same product is a 409, so two cards would just fight).
  - For the POST I send **this order's `line.id`**, not the `orderLineId` eligibility returns — the block sits under this purchase, and the server derives the product from the line either way.
  
  The section renders nothing while the eligibility answers are in flight, and nothing if none of the products is reviewable (a 401 or `NOT_PURCHASED` lands there), so a "Reviews" heading never appears over an empty list.
  
  ## The outbound `?review=1` link
  
  Removed as a link, kept as a control. Two reasons: (1) **`review=1` was already dead** — nothing in the app reads it; `ProductDetail` never opens its Reviews tab from the query string, so the pill dumped the buyer on a product page to go find the tab themselves. (2) The design keeps a pill in that row but its `onReview` just expands the order (`openId: o.id`). So it is now `<button type="button" onClick={onToggle}>` with the identical `PILL` classes, star icon and "Write a review" label — same pixels, and it now lands on the block that actually does the job.
  
  I kept the label static. The design varies it ("Review these items" / "Finish your reviews" / "Your review") off a reviewed-count, which would mean an eligibility request per product for every *collapsed* order in the list. Not worth it for a label; flagged, not built.

  1. picking a rating opens the editor (textarea, Post review, Cancel, `0/500`, `4 of 5`, hint gone)
  2. submitting writes the review and the published state renders (body, `Edit review`, `Posted …`, `5 of 5`, editor gone)
  3. editing a published review sends a PATCH — asserts both the new text/rating and that a `PATCH /api/v1/reviews/{id}` actually went out (recorded via `server.events`)
  4. a refused POST (403) surfaces in `role="alert"` **and the draft survives** — the editor stays open rather than vanishing
  5. an order still in transit gets no Reviews heading and no star row

  - `pnpm exec tsc -b` — clean (an earlier run showed 5 TS6133s in another agent's `src/pages/seller/StoreSettings.test.tsx`; gone on re-run, none ever in my paths)
  - `pnpm exec vitest run src/features/orders src/features/reviews` — **2 files, 13 tests passed** (5 new + the 8 existing review-flow tests, untouched)
  - `pnpm exec oxlint src/features/orders src/features/reviews` — exit 0, no findings. Whole-repo `oxlint` still reports **8 warnings**, baseline held.
  
  ## Found, deliberately not fixed
  - **`Review` has no `updatedAt`/`edited`**, so the published footer always reads "Posted <date>". The design says "Updated <date>" after an edit; I did not invent a field to get it. Noted in a comment in the component.
  - **PATCH's 422** names `rating` in an `errors[]` array with no `detail`, so `apiErrorMessage` falls back to the title ("Validation failed"). The UI can't produce an out-of-range rating (five radios), so this is unreachable from the block; a general `errors[]`-aware message would be a separate change across every form.

Gap 12 done your way and pushed (`9ee48d2`) — **483 tests / 55 files**, lint at 8, build clean.

The key move for making "derived" actually hold: `UpdateSellerOrder.status` no longer refs `OrderStatus`. Adding `REFUNDED` to that enum would have made it writable by definition, so there's now a narrower `SellerOrderTransition` — `PACKED | SHIPPED | CANCELLED` — and the endpoint answers `422` to anyone trying to declare `REFUNDED`. The type system and the server both refuse it, rather than relying on a transition rule that a future caller could talk its way around.

Buyer and seller derive it from the same predicate, so they can't disagree about whether an order was refunded. A `REPLACEMENT_SENT` deliberately leaves the fulfilment status alone — a replacement isn't a refund.

I checked the tests against the un-derived code first: removing the predicate fails the one that matters. The 422 test also asserts the order is untouched by the rejected attempt, not just that the call failed.

**One thing I've left for whoever writes the backend**, recorded in the handoff rather than silently decided: a **partial** refund currently flips the whole order to `REFUNDED`. That's the simplest honest reading, but if your real data has part-refunds it's wrong — it's one predicate to change, and the alternative (keep the fulfilment status, let the refund badge carry it) is written down beside it.

**That leaves gap 8 as the only outstanding item** — the status tab strips with counts and bucket values on Seller Orders, Seller Refunds and My Orders. The `facets` contract is in place, and the Refunded tab the design wants on Seller Orders now has a status to filter on. Want me to start it?

Good catch, and it turned out to be worse than the text. The header branched on "is *anyone* signed in" and treated every session as a buyer, so it was wrong for a seller **two different ways**:

- **What you saw (demo mode):** the portal signs a seller in locally and never sets a cookie, so the server reports nobody — and the header pitched "Sell on Amezo" to someone already selling.
- **What production would do:** with a real cookie, `session.data` is truthy, so a signed-in seller got the **buyer avatar linking to `/account`** — the buyer account page, with their email and buyer order history. I confirmed that by rendering the header with a seller session: the only account link it offered was `Your account → /account`.

It now reads `identityType`. A seller gets **Seller dashboard** → `/seller/dashboard`, a buyer keeps the avatar, a visitor still gets the pitch. The local-flag fallback applies **only** when the server reports no session at all — that's what makes demo mode work — and a real buyer cookie beats a leftover flag, so a stale `localStorage` entry can't claim sellerhood. There's a test for that precedence.

Two things worth knowing:

- **One of my tests failed for a good reason and I changed it rather than force it.** I'd written a demo-mode test asserting a local flag alone means "seller" — it failed, because with a live backend the provider *evicts* that flag. That's correct production behaviour, so the test now asserts the eviction instead, and I verified demo mode in a real browser rather than faking it in jsdom.
- **The footer still says "Sell on Amezo"** and links to sign-in. I left it: it's a static marketing link in a footer nav rather than the header's account slot, and it wasn't what you reported. Say the word if it should follow suit.

Pushed as `c183cd2`. Lint **7 → 4**.

On your re-render concern specifically — the split is render-neutral by construction, and I checked that rather than assuming it. `git diff -M` on the provider shows every deleted line moved out and **nothing inside `CartProvider` changed**: both `createContext` calls, the `useMemo` around `state` and around `actions`, the `useCallback`'d `open`/`close`, and the `<CartActionsContext>` → `<CartStateContext>` nesting order are verbatim. The three hooks moved unedited, so `useCartActions` still subscribes to actions alone.

But "unchanged" is a weaker guarantee than it looked, because **nothing was testing the boundary**. It lived in a comment and in `useAddToCart`'s docstring; merging the two contexts, or dropping the actions `useMemo`, would re-render all sixteen product cards on every quantity change *and pass all 508 tests*. So I added `CartSubscriptions.test.tsx`:

- a dispatch-only consumer does not re-render when lines change or the drawer opens — asserted both directly via `useCartActions` and through `useAddToCart`, the hook a real card calls;
- a reader (`useCartState`) does re-render;
- the actions object keeps one identity for the provider's life — the mechanism the bail-out depends on.

Render counts are compared against a recorded baseline rather than absolute numbers, so they hold under StrictMode's double render too. I confirmed both regressions fail all three tests: removing the actions `useMemo`, and merging the two contexts into one.

On the "don't delay unrelated components" half — one real change beyond the move: `CartState.lines` was `ReturnType<typeof loadCart>`, which would have dragged `storage.ts` into the context module just to name a type. It's `CartLine[]` now, same type. A dispatch-only consumer therefore no longer reaches `cartReducer` or `storage` through its import. To be precise about what that buys: the app builds to a single 1.18 MB chunk, so there's no lazy-load boundary and **nothing about load timing changes either way** — the benefit is module-init and fast refresh, not first paint.

Verified: 511 tests / 57 files, 5 e2e, `tsc` and build clean.

Remaining 4 warnings: `badge.tsx`, `button.tsx`, `tabs.tsx` (shadcn's `cva` variants exported beside the component — splitting these means editing vendored files, which cuts against your "use the official components" rule), and the pre-existing `set-state-in-effect` in `SellerAuthProvider.tsx:72`, which is genuinely synchronising with the session query settling.

Pushed as `6efd42a`. Since PR #15 merged, I restarted the branch from `main` rather than stacking on merged history — this is one commit on top of `bccd1ae`.

**Only the content scrolls.** The shell was `min-h-screen` with no overflow containment, so a long table scrolled the whole document and took the sidebar with it. It's now `h-screen overflow-hidden`, with the banners above a single scrolling well and the sidebar a sibling of it.

One thing here was not obvious and cost the first attempt. After the containment change, the document *still* scrolled — 1403px scrollHeight, `window.scrollTo(0,400)` still moved it — while every visible element fitted. The cause: Tailwind's `sr-only` is `position: absolute`, and the portal is full of it (every dashboard row action carries one naming what it acts on). With no positioned ancestor those spans resolve against the initial containing block, which an *unpositioned* `overflow-hidden` does not clip. Adding `relative` to the well makes it their containing block. Worth knowing because the visual result looked correct at the point it was still broken — only measuring caught it.

**Strips use the width.** `TabsList` ships `inline-flex w-fit` and triggers were `flex-none`, so six tabs stopped two-thirds across. Now `w-full` + `grow basis-[7.5rem]`: 1152 of 1152 available on both Orders and Refunds. I left off `min-w-0` deliberately so a long label like "Ready for pickup" wraps the strip rather than squashing — at 1024px it wraps to two rows with nothing clipped, at 1280px it's one row.

**Gutter.** Every page under the outlet was a bare flex column with no padding, flush against the sidebar border and the window edge. The well carries `p-6`, matching `SellerAddProduct` — the one page that had its own, which I stripped so they don't stack to 48px.

Scope held to four files, all seller-portal: the layout, its test, `FacetTabs` (used only by Seller Orders and Refunds), and that one `p-6`.

**One consequence you should know about:** at 1024px the orders table no longer fits the narrowed content box, so it falls back to the horizontal scroller shadcn's `Table` already wraps it in — reachable, but it's a scroll where it previously fitted. From 1280px up it fits. That's the cost of the gutter at that width; say the word if you'd rather the gutter narrow below 1280 instead.

Verified by screenshot at 1440×900 across dashboard, orders, refunds, products and add-product, and by measurement: sidebar and banner stay at y=0 while the well scrolls, `window.scrollTo(0,500)` leaves `scrollY` at 0, and document scrollHeight equals the viewport. Two structural regression tests added. 513 tests / 57 files, 5 e2e, `tsc`, build and lint (4) clean.

Two audits are still running. But I've established environment facts that materially change this plan, and they're worth surfacing before two agents spend hours on it.

## Blocking finding: the backend is far emptier than the contract implies

The `/api/v1` surface I built earlier in this session is **contract + MSW mock only — there is no backend behind any of it.** Verified live against a running instance:

| Path | Result |
|---|---|
| `/categories` | 200 |
| `/api/v1/categories` | 401 |
| `/api/v1/sellers/me/metrics` | 401 |
| `/api/v1/orders/facets` | 401 |

Every existing controller is **unprefixed** (`/products`, `/sellers/me/products`, `/sessions/current`). Nothing under `/api/v1` is routed, so it falls through to `anyRequest().denyAll()`. There is also **no refunds domain, no metrics domain, and no store-profile domain** in the backend at all — the live schema has 12 tables and none of them are `refund_request`, `store_profile`, or any metrics rollup.

So "implement backend first" for the Dashboard means building a metrics domain from scratch, not filling gaps.

## Blocking finding: backend tests cannot run in this container

Docker's daemon isn't available, and all 15 backend integration tests declare their own `@Container PostgreSQLContainer`. Measured baseline: **41 tests, 15 errors, 26 pass** — every error is "Could not find a valid Docker environment", not a code fault.

I did **not** try to work around this by repointing the tests, because each test class owns its container (no shared base), so it'd mean editing 15 files and breaking real CI — squarely the "no opportunistic refactoring" you ruled out.

Instead I stood up a real PostgreSQL 16 on `127.0.0.1:5433` and confirmed the app boots against it with **no code changes** (the datasource is env-overridable; Flyway applied all 16 migrations). So new backend endpoints can be verified live with real HTTP calls, even though Testcontainers tests can only be *written*, not *run*, here.

## What I've done so far

- Audited repo structure, backend endpoint inventory, schema, security config, and toolchain.
- Found the design files at `/tmp/claude-0/design2/` — including `Seller Dashboard.dc.html`, `Seller Products.dc.html`, `Product Form.dc.html`.
- Wrote a shared brief capturing these facts (prefix rule, ArchUnit feature-boundary constraint, verification commands, lint baseline of 4) so neither agent re-derives them.
- Identified one genuine shared-file conflict: both agents would edit `SecurityConfig.java`. A single `/api/v1/sellers/me/**` rule covers both, which I'll apply myself rather than let them race.

## Buyer My Orders — confirmation (item 3, complete)

**Not complete.** The React side is close to done and genuinely good — tabs with live counts, URL-driven filters, lazy detail fetch, refund progress panel, inline review editor, ~24 passing tests. But **all three endpoints it depends on are unimplemented**: `GET /api/v1/orders`, `/orders/facets`, `/orders/{orderId}`. `OrderController` is checkout-only. In production the page renders a skeleton, then "Couldn't load your orders". `PATCH /reviews/{id}` (Edit review) is also missing. UI gaps vs design: `Invoice`, `Print return label`, the refund panel's secondary action, the header summary line, the empty-state reset, and `Track package` tracks nothing. **No work started, as instructed.**

Dashboard and Products audits are still running — I'll report those and launch Agents A and B when they land.

One thing worth your call now: **Products already has a working backend under unprefixed paths.** "Backend first" there may mean re-exposing it under `/api/v1` rather than building new. I'll confirm the exact gap from the audit, but if you'd rather Agent B leave the existing product endpoints alone and only add what's genuinely missing, say so — it's the difference between a small change and a duplicated API surface.
