    - Widget grid (243): `repeat(auto-fit, minmax(340px, 1fr)); gap:16px`.
  
  ### 1. Demo banner (60–62)
  `role="status"`, conditional on `showDemoBanner`. Text verbatim: **"Demo mode — authentication is mocked."**
  
  ### 2. Page header (66–83)
  - `<h1>`: **"Aurora Audio"** (line 68) — the *store name*, not the word "Dashboard".
  - Sub-line: `{{ rangeCaption }}` = `short(first) + ' – ' + short(last) + ' · compared with the previous ' + current.length + ' days'` (line 473). e.g. **"Sep 1 – Sep 25 · compared with the previous 25 days"**.
  - **Range switcher** (71–82): pill group of 3 buttons, active one filled orange. Labels verbatim (379–385): **"This month"**, **"Last 30 days"**, **"Last 7 days"**. Windows (387–394): `d7` = last 7 vs the 7 before; `d30` = last 30 vs the 30 before; `month` = calendar month-to-date vs the same number of days of the previous month.
  
  ### 3. KPI tiles — exactly 4 (85–110, data at 480–485)
  Each tile: small label, 28px value, then one of {green up-arrow pill | red down-arrow pill | orange flag pill} carrying `delta`, then a muted `caption`.
  
  | # | `label` (verbatim) | `value` | `delta` | `caption` (verbatim) |
  |---|---|---|---|---|
  | 1 | **Revenue** | `money(revenue)` | `±N.N%` | **"vs previous period"** |
  | 2 | **Orders** | integer count | `±N.N%` | **"$X average order"** |
  | 3 | **Stock alerts** | `"5"` (hardcoded) | **"2 out of stock"** (orange flag, `isFlag:true`) | **"3 below 10 units"** |
  | 4 | **Conversion rate** | `conv.toFixed(2) + '%'` (**2 dp**) | `±N.N%` | **"N,NNN store views"** |
  
  `pct()` (402–407) returns `'—'` when previous is falsy.
  
  ### 4. Chart band — 4 reorderable `<section>`s (112–241)
  Each chart panel header (115–136) carries **five** things:
  1. Drag grip, `title="Drag to reorder"` (117–119)
  2. `<h2>{{ slot.title }}` (121)
  3. **`<p>{{ slot.subtitle }}`** (122) — a second line under the title
  4. **`<span>{{ slot.value }}`** (126) — a 20px bold number on the right of the header
  5. Two icon buttons: `aria-label="Move chart earlier"` / `"Move chart later"`, disabled at the ends (128–133)
  
  Panel is `draggable` with `onDragStart/onDragOver/onDrop/onDragEnd` (114); drag-over reorders live (519–525). Default order `['revenue','orders','top','category']` (356).
  
  Titles / subtitles / header values (500–505), verbatim:
  
  | key | `title` | `subtitle` | `value` |
  |---|---|---|---|
  | revenue | **"Revenue"** | **"Peak day $X"** | `money(revenue)` |
  | orders | **"Orders per day"** | **"N.N per day"** | `String(orders)` |
  | top | **"Top products by revenue"** | **"Top 5 of 6 products selling"** | `''` |
  | category | **"Sales by category"** | **"4 categories selling"** | `''` |
  
  **4a. Revenue chart** (138–153): 190px SVG area+line, `viewBox 0 0 100 40`, 3 horizontal gridlines at y=3 / 21.5 / 40, orange line `#f4750f` + `#f4750f14` fill. Below it a 3-up axis row: `{{ axisStart }}` / `{{ axisMid }}` / `{{ axisEnd }}` (148–152) = first / middle / last date as `"Sep 1"` (496–498). No tooltip.
  
  **4b. Orders chart** (155–167): 190px flex row of bars, one per day, `title="{{ bar.title }}"` = **"Sep 3: 12 orders"** (494) as a native tooltip, min height 6%. Below it a **2-up** axis row: `axisStart` / `axisEnd` only.
  
  **4c. Top products table** (169–209): fixed grid `26px minmax(0,1fr) 92px 56px 74px`.
  - Header cells verbatim: **`#`**, **`Product`**, **`Revenue`**, **`Share`**, **`vs prev`** (171–175).
  - 5 rows (`topProducts`, 532–548): `rank` (1-based), `title`, second line **"{{ p.unitsCount }} units · {{ p.aov }} each"** (182), `revenue` as money, `share` as `"29%"`, and a `vs prev` cell that is green-up / red-down / the literal word **"Flat"** (199–201) when |change| ≤ 0.5%.
  - Footer row (205–208): left **"Top 5 share of $X"** (`topFooterLabel`, 530), right `topFooterValue` (bold).
  - **No links** on product rows.
  
  **4d. Sales by category** (211–238): 168×168 donut, `viewBox 140 140`, r=54, stroke-width 20, rotated −90°, grey track ring under coloured arcs built from `stroke-dasharray`/`stroke-dashoffset`. Centre overlay: label **"Total"** + `{{ revenueTotal }}`. Legend to the right, one entry per category: colour swatch, `label`, `share` (`"29%"`), and beneath it `revenue` as money (226–235). Palette is fixed by category name (469): Headphones `#f4750f`, Earbuds `#f79a4d`, Speakers `#fabf8c`, Wearables `#8a5a2b`, Accessories `#5d4033`.
  
  ### 5. Widget band — 4 reorderable `<section>`s (243–343)
  Header (246–269): grip, `<h2>{{ w.title }}`, then either an `<a href>{{ w.linkLabel }}` **or** a muted `{{ w.note }}`, then the two move buttons (`aria-label="Move widget earlier"` / `"Move widget later"`). Default order `['ship','lowStock','recent','refunds']` (357).
  
  Meta (564–569):
  
  | key | `title` | link / note |
  |---|---|---|
  | ship | **"Orders to ship"** | link **"All orders"** → Seller Orders |
  | lowStock | **"Low stock"** | link **"All products"** → Seller Products |
  | recent | **"Recent orders"** | link **"All orders"** → Seller Orders |
  | refunds | **"Refund requests"** | note **"2 open · $311"** (no link) |
  
  **5a. Orders to ship** (271–288, data 597–602) — 4 rows: monospace `shortId` (`"c41d9a70"`), buyer name, second line **`items`** as text — **"1 × Aurora One Wireless Headphones"**, **"2 × Aurora One, 2 × Travel Case"** — then `age` (**"3 days"**, **"Today"**) as a red pill when `isOverdue` else plain muted text, then an orange **"Ship"** link.
  
  **5b. Low stock** (290–306, data 603–609) — 5 rows: product `title`, second line **`sku`** (**"AUR-BP-BLK"**), then either a red pill **"Out of stock"** or an orange pill `"3 left"`, then an outline **"Restock"** link.
  
  **5c. Recent orders** (308–325, data 610–616) — 5 rows: monospace `shortId`, `buyer`, `total` as money, then one status pill: filled-orange **"PLACED"**, grey **"SHIPPED"**, outline **"DELIVERED"**. No row action.
  
  **5d. Refund requests** (327–341, data 617–621) — 2 rows: `buyer`, second line **`reason`** = **"Item arrived damaged · order 8ca07d31"**, then `amount` as money, then an outline **"Review"** link. Empty copy verbatim: **"Nothing waiting on a decision."** (339).
  
  ### 6. Interactions, complete list
  - Range switch (3 buttons) → recomputes every KPI, both charts, the axes, the top-product deltas and the category revenues.
  - Chart reorder: drag grip **and** ←/→ buttons; in-memory only, not persisted.
  - Widget reorder: same, separate order.
  - Links: sidebar Products/Orders; "All orders" ×2; "All products"; "Ship"; "Restock"; "Review". All point at other design pages.
  - Native `title` tooltips: order bars, both drag grips.

  ## Part 3 — Backend as built
  
  ### The contract (fixture.yaml)
  
  **`GET /api/v1/sellers/me/metrics`** (fixture.yaml:1399-1430) — `operationId: getSellerMetrics`. Query: `from` (date, **required**), `to` (date, **required**), `interval` (enum `day|week|month`, default `day`). 200 → `SellerMetrics`.
  - `SellerMetrics` (2650-2665): required `from`, `to`, `currency`, `totals`, `series`; optional nullable `previousTotals`.
  - `MetricTotals` (2641-2649): `views` (int), `orders` (int), `revenue` (num), `conversionRate` (num), `averageOrderValue` (num) — all required.
  - `MetricPoint` (2633-2640): `date`, `views`, `orders`, `revenue` — all required.
  
  **`GET /api/v1/sellers/me/metrics/top-products`** (1431-1459) — `operationId: getSellerTopProducts`. Query: `from`, `to` (required), `limit` (int, default 5). 200 → `TopProduct[]`.
  - `TopProduct` (2666-2680): required `productId` (uuid), `productRef`, `title`, `units`, `revenue`, `share`; optional `thumbnailUrl` (nullable), `previousRevenue` (nullable — *"Null when it did not sell then - which is a fact, not a zero."*).
  
  **`GET /api/v1/sellers/me/metrics/category-breakdown`** (1460-1485) — `operationId: getSellerCategoryBreakdown`. Query: `from`, `to` (required only). 200 → `CategoryShare[]`.
  - `CategoryShare` (2681-2688): `category` (→ `Category` = `{slug, name}`, 1637-1646), `revenue`, `units`, `share` — all required.
  
  Frontend call sites: `frontend/src/features/seller-metrics/api/useSellerMetrics.ts:28-33` (always sends `interval: 'day'`), `:42-47`, `:56-61`.
  
  ### Endpoint-by-endpoint: what exists in the backend
  
  **Nothing exists. For any of the three.**
  
  `grep -rni "metric" /home/user/amezo/backend/` returns **0 matches** — no file, class, method, comment, route, property or migration in the entire backend module mentions metrics.
  
  | Endpoint | Controller | Service | Repository query | DTO |
  |---|---|---|---|---|
  | `GET /api/v1/sellers/me/metrics` | **none** | **none** | **none** | **none** |
  | `.../metrics/top-products` | **none** | **none** | **none** | **none** |
  | `.../metrics/category-breakdown` | **none** | **none** | **none** | **none** |
  
  Also definitively absent: `SellerMetrics`, `MetricTotals`, `MetricPoint`, `TopProduct`, `CategoryShare` — zero matches for any of these names under `backend/src/`. No `metrics` package exists (`backend/src/main/java/com/arkindustries/amezo/` contains exactly: `catalog`, `common`, `config`, `identity`, `orders`, `reviews`).
  
  **Wider finding, load-bearing for this work:** `grep -rn "api/v1" backend/src/main/java` returns **0 matches**, and `application.yml` sets no `server.servlet.context-path`. The backend serves only the unversioned paths (`/sellers/me/products`, `/sellers/me/orders`, …). The frontend's `apiClient` base URL is bare `http://localhost:8080` (`frontend/src/lib/api/client.ts:57`), so **every `/api/v1/**` path in the fixture is unimplemented** — that includes the dashboard's three widget feeds (`/api/v1/sellers/me/products`, `/api/v1/sellers/me/orders`, `/api/v1/sellers/me/refund-requests`) as well as the metrics trio. Note also `SecurityConfig.java:138` matches `"/sellers/me/**"`, which does **not** match `/api/v1/sellers/me/**`; a new `/api/v1` route would fall through to `anyRequest().denyAll()` (`:150`) and 403 until a matcher is added. This is the exact failure mode already documented in that file at `:91-94` and `:132-137`.
  
  Refunds are likewise entirely absent: `grep -rni refund backend/src/main` = **0 matches**, and there is no refund migration.
  
  ### Tables these endpoints would read from
  
  Schema is Flyway, `backend/src/main/resources/db/migration/`, V1–V16.
  
  - **`orders`** — `V9__create_orders.sql:9-18`: `id UUID PK`, `buyer_identity_id UUID NOT NULL FK`, `seller_id UUID FK` (**relaxed to nullable** by `V12:9` — no longer reliable, see below), `buyer_email_snapshot VARCHAR(255)`, `status VARCHAR(10) CHECK IN ('PLACED','SHIPPED','DELIVERED')`, `tracking_number VARCHAR(100)`, `shipped_at TIMESTAMPTZ`, **`placed_at TIMESTAMPTZ NOT NULL DEFAULT now()`**. Indexes on `buyer_identity_id`, `seller_id`. `V12` adds `buyer_phone` + 15 shipping/billing address columns. **There is no order-level total or currency column.**
  - **`order_line`** — `V10__create_order_line.sql:6-19`: `id UUID PK`, `order_id UUID NOT NULL FK→orders(id)`, `offer_id UUID NOT NULL FK→offer(id)`, **`product_id_snapshot UUID NOT NULL`**, `variant_id_snapshot UUID NOT NULL`, **`seller_id_snapshot UUID NOT NULL`**, **`unit_price_snapshot NUMERIC(10,2) NOT NULL`**, **`quantity INTEGER NOT NULL CHECK (quantity > 0)`**, **`created_at TIMESTAMPTZ NOT NULL DEFAULT now()`**. Indexes: `idx_order_line_order_id`, `idx_order_line_seller_id_snapshot`.
  - **`product`** — `V5:1-18` + `V14` + `V15`: `id`, `seller_id FK→seller(id)`, `title VARCHAR(255)`, `brand_name`, `description`, `search_vector`, `created_at`, `category_id UUID NOT NULL FK→category(id)` (V14), `slug VARCHAR(255) NOT NULL UNIQUE` (V15). The free-text `category` column was **dropped** by `V14`.
  - **`category`** — `V14:9-21`: `id`, `slug VARCHAR(100) UNIQUE`, `name VARCHAR(100) UNIQUE`, `active BOOLEAN`, `position INTEGER`, `created_at`. Seeded with 12 rows. This is the source for `CategoryShare.category` = `{slug, name}`.
  - **`variant`** — `V6`: `id`, `product_id FK`, `label`, **`sku VARCHAR(100) NOT NULL UNIQUE`**, `created_at`.
  - **`offer`** — `V7`: `id`, `variant_id UUID UNIQUE FK`, `price NUMERIC(10,2)`, `stock_qty INTEGER`, `created_at`.
  - **`seller`** — `V1`: `id`, `email UNIQUE`, `full_name`, `created_at`. **No store name / handle column**, so the design's `<h1>Aurora Audio</h1>` has no backing column either.
  
  **Revenue/orders time series: the columns exist.** `order_line.created_at` (and/or `orders.placed_at`) is the timestamp; money is `SUM(unit_price_snapshot * quantity)`; the seller scope is `order_line.seller_id_snapshot`. `idx_order_line_seller_id_snapshot` already supports the filter, though there is **no composite index on `(seller_id_snapshot, created_at)`** for a time-bucketed scan.
  
  Two caveats that must not be glossed over:
  1. **Do not join on `orders.seller_id`.** `V12__add_checkout_fields_to_orders.sql:1-9` relaxed it to nullable and states checkout no longer sets it; `SellerOrderService`'s class comment (`:24-38`) and the regression test `SellerOrderApiTest.java:105-113` both confirm real orders have `seller_id IS NULL`. Ownership is **per line**, via `seller_id_snapshot`.
  2. **Which timestamp.** `order_line.created_at` and `orders.placed_at` both default to `now()` at checkout and will agree today, but only `placed_at` is the order's business event. A bucket-by-day over either needs an explicit timezone decision — the frontend builds `from`/`to` from the browser's **local** calendar day (`SellerDashboard.tsx:57-69`), while the columns are `TIMESTAMPTZ`.
  
  ### Blockers in the contract that the schema cannot satisfy
  
  - **`views` and `conversionRate` (both required on `MetricTotals`, `views` required on `MetricPoint`) have no source.** There is no view/impression/visit/pageview table, column or counter anywhere: `grep -i "view\|impression\|visit"` over `db/migration/` matches only `search_vector` and index names. Product/store view tracking does not exist. These two fields cannot be computed from the current schema — they need a new table (e.g. `product_view` / `store_view` with `seller_id`, `product_id`, `occurred_at`) plus a write path, or the contract has to make them nullable.
  - **`currency` (required on `SellerMetrics`) has no source.** `grep -rni currency backend/src/main` = **0 matches**; no column on `orders`, `order_line`, `offer` or `seller`. It would have to be a hardcoded constant.
  - **`TopProduct.thumbnailUrl`** is available via the `image` table + `catalog/ImageUrlResolver.java`, but only through `catalog`'s internals — see the package-boundary rule below.
  - **`TopProduct.productRef`** maps to `product.slug` (V15) — `catalog/api/ProductReferenceResolver.java` already exists for slug↔id.
  
  ### What `TopProduct.previousRevenue` needs
  
  The contract distinguishes "sold nothing in the previous window" (`0`) from "was not measured / did not appear" (`null`), and the frontend depends on it: `changeVsPrevious.ts:29` maps `null` → `"No prior data"` and `:33` maps a measured `0` → `"New"`. So:
  
  1. Compute the **previous window of equal length**: `previousFrom = from - (to - from + 1 day)`, `previousTo = from - 1 day`. (The same rule `SellerMetrics.previousTotals` needs; fixture.yaml:1402-1405 describes it as "the immediately preceding window of equal length".)
  2. Aggregate `SUM(ol.unit_price_snapshot * ol.quantity)` grouped by `ol.product_id_snapshot`, filtered on `ol.seller_id_snapshot = :sellerId` and the previous window's timestamps.
  3. **LEFT JOIN** that map onto the current window's top-N by product id, so a product with no previous-window rows yields Java `null`, not `BigDecimal.ZERO`. This is the only part that is easy to get wrong — a `COALESCE(..., 0)` or a primitive `double` field silently turns every new product into `"New"` where it should read `"No prior data"`. Use `BigDecimal` (nullable), matching `order_line.unit_price_snapshot NUMERIC(10,2)`.
  4. `share` is `productRevenue / windowRevenue` for the **current** window; the frontend also independently sums the rows for the footer (`TopProductsTable.tsx:95`), so the two must agree.
  
  The same pattern covers `SellerMetrics.previousTotals` (nullable when the previous window has no data at all).
  
  ### Backend conventions to follow
  
  **Seller authn/authz.** Two layers, both required:
  1. **Route rule** — `config/SecurityConfig.java:138`: `.requestMatchers("/sellers/me/**").hasRole("SELLER")`. The comment at `:132-137` explains why the namespace is listed once rather than method-by-method. A path outside a listed matcher hits `.anyRequest().denyAll()` (`:150`). **A `/api/v1/...` route needs its own matcher.** Authentication is a session cookie resolved by `identity/SessionCookieAuthenticationFilter.java`, registered at `SecurityConfig.java:155`; 401/403 bodies come from `ProblemDetailAuthenticationEntryPoint` / `ProblemDetailAccessDeniedHandler` (`:152-154`).
  2. **Principal** — the controller/service never reads the SecurityContext directly. It injects `identity.api.CurrentSeller` (`identity/api/CurrentSeller.java`, implemented package-private by `identity/CurrentSellerResolver.java:11-25`) and calls `currentSeller.sellerId()`. Examples: `orders/SellerOrderService.java:44,59,91` and `catalog/SellerProductService.java`.
  
  **Controller shape.** `catalog/SellerProductController.java:36-43`: `@RestController`, constructor injection (no field `@Autowired`), thin — every method delegates to one service call, no logic. `orders/SellerOrderController.java:20-22` adds a class-level `@RequestMapping("/sellers/me/orders")`; `SellerProductController` instead annotates each method. Query params via `@RequestParam(required = false)`, paging via a `Pageable` argument (`SellerOrderController.java:32`; springdoc flattens it — `application.yml` `default-flat-param-object: true`). Bodies are `@Valid @RequestBody`. Errors are thrown as `common/exception/NotFoundException` / `ConflictException` and rendered by `common/exception/ApiExceptionHandler.java`. **404, not 403, when a resource is not the caller's** — `SellerOrderService.java:98-103` documents that convention.
  
  **Package layout.** `com.arkindustries.amezo.<feature>` with `<feature>/` (entities, repositories, services, controllers), `<feature>/dto/` (wire records), `<feature>/api/` (the only cross-feature-importable surface). Enforced by `backend/src/test/java/com/arkindustries/amezo/architecture/PackageBoundaryTest.java:40-73` — an ArchUnit rule over `FEATURES = {"catalog","orders","reviews","identity"}` that **fails the build** if one feature touches another's internals. **This matters here:** metrics needs order money (orders) *and* product title/slug/category (catalog). Either put it in `orders` and add a `catalog.api` query interface for product title/slug/category/thumbnail by id (the existing `catalog/api/ProductVariantSummaryQuery.java` is the template — batched `Map<UUID, String>` lookups, explicitly created for orders' snapshot ids), or add a new `metrics` feature and register it in `FEATURES` at `PackageBoundaryTest.java:43`.
  
  **DTO convention.** Java `record`s in `<feature>/dto/`, one per wire shape, no Lombok, no builders, usually with a class-level doc comment explaining the shape. See `orders/dto/SellerOrderSummaryResponse.java` (flat record of `UUID`/`String`/`Instant`/`BigDecimal`/enum) and `catalog/dto/CategoryResponse.java` (`record CategoryResponse(String slug, String name)` — this is already exactly the contract's `Category`, so `CategoryShare` should nest it). Money is `BigDecimal`, ids are `UUID`, timestamps are `Instant`, enums are the domain enum (`OrderStatus`) serialised by name.
  
  **Test convention.** Integration tests over the real HTTP surface, no unit-test-the-service layer:
  - `orders/SellerOrderApiTest.java:42-45` — `@SpringBootTest` + `@AutoConfigureMockMvc` + `@Testcontainers`, with `@Container @ServiceConnection static PostgreSQLContainer<?> postgres = new PostgreSQLContainer<>("postgres:16-alpine")`. Flyway runs against the container, so tests exercise the real schema.
  - Repositories are `@Autowired` and rows are seeded directly (`:56-84`, `:114-120`), then asserted through `mockMvc.perform(get("/sellers/me/orders").cookie(cookie))` with `jsonPath` matchers (`:96-101`).
  - Auth in tests comes from `support/Fixtures.sessionCookie(sessions, IdentityType.SELLER, sellerId)` (`support/Fixtures.java:75-88`) — it mints a `Session` row with the SHA-256 hex token hash the production filter looks up, deliberately bypassing the magic-link round trip. `Fixtures.categoryId(...)` (`:37-46`) and `Fixtures.uniqueSlug(...)` (`:55-57`) satisfy `product.category_id` NOT NULL and `product.slug` UNIQUE.
  - Test names are full sentences: `listMineOnlyReturnsTheCallingSellersOrdersWithComputedTotals`, `findsAnOrderWithNoOrderLevelSellerIdSetPurelyFromTheLinesSellerSnapshot`. Cross-seller isolation ("only the calling seller's rows") is asserted in essentially every seller-scoped test — a metrics test suite should do the same, plus window-boundary cases (a row exactly on `from`, exactly on `to`, one day outside) and a `previousRevenue` null-vs-zero case.
  - Pure-logic helpers get plain JUnit tests without Spring: `catalog/SlugsTest.java`, `common/CountryCatalogTest.java`.
  
  ### Summary of backend work implied
  
  1. Decide the `/api/v1` story (context path vs. per-route prefix) and add the matching `SecurityConfig` matcher — otherwise every new route 403s.
  2. New `metrics` feature (or `orders`-hosted) with controller + service + a native/JPQL aggregate repository over `order_line` joined to `orders`, scoped by `seller_id_snapshot`, bucketed by day.
  3. A `catalog.api` query for product title / slug / category / thumbnail by snapshot id, to stay inside the ArchUnit boundary.
  4. Resolve the three contract fields with no data behind them: `views`, `conversionRate`, `currency`.
  5. Nullable `BigDecimal` for `previousRevenue` and `previousTotals`, left-joined, never coalesced to zero.

Log committed and pushed (`2921083`); tree is clean. The Dashboard audit landed and surfaced a decision that's genuinely yours, not mine.

## Dashboard: the backend can't honour the contract as written

The metrics backend is **entirely absent** — `grep -rni "metric"` across the whole backend returns zero matches. That part is just work, and the schema supports most of it: `order_line` carries `seller_id_snapshot`, `unit_price_snapshot`, `quantity` and `created_at`, which is everything a revenue/orders time series needs.

But three required contract fields have **no data source at all**:

| Field | Required by | Source in schema |
|---|---|---|
| `views` | `MetricTotals` + every `MetricPoint` | **None.** No view/impression/visit table, column or counter exists anywhere. |
| `conversionRate` | `MetricTotals` | **None** — it's derived from `views`. |
| `currency` | `SellerMetrics` | **None.** Zero matches in the backend. |

This collides directly with your standing "no fake data to make screens look complete" rule. The design's fourth KPI tile is **"Conversion rate"** with a **"12,604 store views"** caption, so it isn't a fringe field. Agent A has three options and I don't think I should pick for you:

1. **Build view tracking** — new table + a write path on the buyer-facing product/store pages. Genuinely out of Agent A's "dashboard only" scope, and it touches the buyer app.
2. **Make `views`/`conversionRate` nullable in the contract** and have the KPI tile render an explicit "not tracked yet" state. Honest, cheap, and the tile already has vocabulary for absent data (`No prior data`).
3. **Hardcode a constant.** Fastest, and exactly what you told me not to do.

I'd take **option 2** and leave `currency` as a documented constant (there's genuinely one currency in the system today). Say the word and Agent A proceeds on that basis.

## Two other findings worth knowing before the agents start

**The ArchUnit rule constrains where metrics can live.** `PackageBoundaryTest` fails the build if one feature imports another's internals. Metrics needs order money (`orders`) *and* product title/slug/category (`catalog`). So it must either live in `orders` with a new `catalog.api` query interface, or become a new `metrics` feature registered in that test. Not a blocker — just not a free choice.

**`previousRevenue` has a trap.** The contract distinguishes "sold nothing previously" (`0`) from "wasn't there" (`null`), and the frontend renders those differently (`New` vs `No prior data`). A `COALESCE(...,0)` or a primitive `double` silently collapses them. I've flagged it in the agent brief.

## Status

- **Buyer My Orders** — audit complete, reported last turn, no work started. Confirmed **not** complete: all three endpoints unimplemented.
- **Dashboard** — audit complete. Frontend is largely built with 8 concrete gaps vs design (store-name `<h1>`, chart subtitles, chart header values, `Conversion rate` label/2dp, ship-queue item names, low-stock SKU, refund buyer/reason). Backend is zero.
- **Products** — audit still running.

I've held off launching Agents A and B deliberately. Agent A's first task is the metrics backend, and that decision determines its shape — starting it now would mean redoing the work if you pick differently. Tell me which option for `views`/`conversionRate` and I'll launch both agents immediately (Agent B isn't blocked; I'll start it as soon as its audit lands regardless).

  ---
  
  # Part 3 — Existing shared drawer infrastructure
  
  ### 3.1 Primitives in `frontend/src/components/ui/`
  
  `frontend/src/components/ui/components.json` → `"$schema": "https://ui.shadcn.com/schema.json"`, style `radix-nova` — **everything under `components/ui/` is shadcn/ui**.
  
  - **`sheet.tsx`** — the side-drawer primitive. Built on `@radix-ui/react-dialog` (`sheet.tsx:1`). Exported names (`sheet.tsx:88-99`):
    `Sheet`, `SheetClose`, `SheetContent`, `SheetDescription`, `SheetFooter`, `SheetHeader`, `SheetOverlay`, `SheetPortal`, `SheetTitle`, `SheetTrigger`.
    `SheetContent` takes a `side?: 'top'|'right'|'bottom'|'left'` (default `'right'`) and always renders its own close `X` (`:55-58`). Default width `w-3/4 sm:max-w-sm`.
  - **`dialog.tsx`** — centred modal. Built on the `radix-ui` meta-package (`dialog.tsx:13`). Exported names (`dialog.tsx:165-176`): `Dialog`, `DialogClose`, `DialogContent`, `DialogDescription`, `DialogFooter`, `DialogHeader`, `DialogOverlay`, `DialogPortal`, `DialogTitle`, `DialogTrigger`. `DialogContent` has a `showCloseButton?: boolean`; `DialogFooter` has its own `showCloseButton?: boolean`.
  - **`drawer.tsx`: does not exist.** There is no `vaul` dependency in `frontend/package.json`.
  - Also present and useful to a compound drawer: `scroll-area.tsx`, `separator.tsx`, `tabs.tsx`, `tooltip.tsx`, `popover.tsx`, `dropdown-menu.tsx`.
  
  ### 3.2 Everywhere a drawer/panel is rendered today
  
  There is exactly **one** drawer component, used by **three** lists, each pairing it with a body component that is also a standalone route.
  
  1. **`frontend/src/features/seller-portal/components/DetailDrawer.tsx`** — the only drawer wrapper (63 lines).
     Props (`:21-35`): `{ open: boolean; onOpenChange: (open: boolean) => void; title: string; description?: string; fullPageTo: string; children: ReactNode }`.
     Internals: `Sheet` → `SheetContent className="flex w-full flex-col gap-0 p-0 sm:max-w-[640px]"` → `SheetHeader` with `SheetTitle`/`SheetDescription` + a `Link ... target="_blank"` labelled **"Full page"** → a scroll container `div.min-h-0.flex-1.overflow-y-auto.px-6.py-5` holding `children`.
     Limitations for absorption: fixed width; no footer slot; no way to set `aria-label`; no view/edit mode; `fullPageTo` is hard-wired to a new tab; header layout is not overridable.
  
  2. **Call sites** (all identical in shape):
     - `frontend/src/pages/seller/SellerProducts.tsx:397-409` — `title={openRow?.title ?? 'Edit product'}`, `description="<category> · <n> variants"`, `fullPageTo={/seller/products/<id>}`, child `<ProductFormPanel productId={...} />`
     - `frontend/src/pages/seller/SellerOrders.tsx:351-363` — `title={"Order <reference>"}`, `description={recipientName}`, `fullPageTo={/seller/orders/<id>}`, child `<SellerOrderPanel orderId={...} />`
     - `frontend/src/pages/seller/SellerRefunds.tsx:268-280` — `title={"Request <reference>"}`, `description={orderReference}`, `fullPageTo={/seller/refunds/<id>}`, child `<RefundDecisionPanel refundRequestId={...} />`
  
  3. **Panel bodies** — all three take a single id prop and carry no chrome, by design:
     - `frontend/src/features/seller-portal/components/ProductFormPanel.tsx:46` — `ProductFormPanel({ productId }: { productId: string })`
     - `frontend/src/features/seller-portal/components/SellerOrderPanel.tsx:69` — `SellerOrderPanel({ orderId }: { orderId: string })`
     - `frontend/src/features/refunds/components/RefundDecisionPanel.tsx:122` — `RefundDecisionPanel({ refundRequestId }: { refundRequestId: string })`
     Each is also mounted standalone by `SellerProductDetail.tsx:24`, `SellerOrderDetail.tsx:25`, `SellerRefundDetail.tsx:22`.
  
  4. **Shared list-page pattern** all three repeat (worth absorbing alongside the drawer): `openId` + `openSnapshot` state, `openRow = rows.find(...) ?? openSnapshot`, and an `onOpenChange` that clears both. See `SellerProducts.tsx:72-73`, `:142`, `:398-403` and the identical blocks in `SellerOrders.tsx` / `SellerRefunds.tsx`.
  
  ### 3.3 Compound-component precedent in this codebase
  
  **There is no dot-notation compound component anywhere.** A repo-wide search for `^[A-Z][A-Za-z]+\.[A-Z][A-Za-z]+ = ` over `src/**/*.{ts,tsx}` returns **zero** matches — no `X.Header = ...`, no `Object.assign(X, {...})`, no exported namespace objects.
  
  The closest precedents to follow:
  
  - **`frontend/src/components/ui/chart.tsx`** — the one shadcn component in the repo that uses `createContext` to share state between sibling sub-components: `ChartContext` at `chart.tsx:27`, provided by `ChartContainer` (`:39`), consumed by `ChartTooltipContent` (`:116`) and `ChartLegendContent` (`:272`), all exported as flat siblings at `:363`. This is the *pattern* a compound drawer should follow (context + flat named exports), just without the dot notation.
  - **Every other shadcn primitive** (`sheet.tsx`, `dialog.tsx`, `tabs.tsx`, `card.tsx`, `table.tsx`) uses flat named sub-component exports with Radix's own context underneath — so `Drawer`, `DrawerHeader`, `DrawerBody`, `DrawerFooter`, `DrawerActions` as flat exports (optionally also attached as `Drawer.Header` etc.) is idiomatic here.
  - Other `createContext` usages, for style reference only: `frontend/src/features/cart/context/CartContext.ts:39-40` (split state/actions contexts + a `useContext` hook that throws when unprovided) and `frontend/src/features/seller-portal/context/SellerAuthContext.ts:24`.
  
  ---
  
  # Part 4 — Backend as built
  
  ### 4.1 Contract (`frontend/openapi/fixture.yaml`) vs `SellerProductController` / `SellerProductService`
  
  Note first: **the backend serves no `/api/v1` prefix at all.** No `server.servlet.context-path` is set in `backend/src/main/resources/application*.yml`, and no controller maps `/api/v1/...` (`@RequestMapping` inventory: `/products`, `/variants`, `/categories`, `/sessions`, `/auth/seller`, `/auth/buyer`, `/orders`, `/sellers/me/orders`, `/checkout`, `/countries`). Every `/api/v1/...` path in the fixture is unimplemented.
  
  | Contract operation (fixture line) | Backend | Verdict |
  |---|---|---|
  | `GET /sellers/me/products` → `SellerProductSummaryPage` (`:486-508`, op `sellerListProducts`) | `SellerProductController.java:86-89` → `SellerProductService.java:112-135` | **IMPLEMENTED** (page/size via `Pageable` only) |
  | `POST /sellers/me/products` → `{id}` (`:509-530`, op `sellerCreateProduct`) | `SellerProductController.java:95-99` → `SellerProductService.java:137-167` | **IMPLEMENTED**, except the contract's `status: [ACTIVE, DRAFT]` field (`fixture.yaml:1964-1966`) — `CreateProductRequest.java` has no `status`, and `create()` never writes one |
  | `GET /sellers/me/products/{productId}` → `SellerProductDetail` (`:685-706`) | `SellerProductController.java:50-53` → `SellerProductService.java:174-215` | **IMPLEMENTED**, except the contract's `status` property (`fixture.yaml:1802`) — `SellerProductDetailResponse.java` has no `status` field |
  | `PATCH /products/{productRef}` → `SellerProductDetail` (`:92-116`, op `sellerUpdateProduct`) | `SellerProductController.java:55-59` → `SellerProductService.java:218-240` | **IMPLEMENTED**, except `status` (`fixture.yaml:1825-1827`) — `UpdateProductRequest.java` has no `status` field and `update()` has no branch for it |
  | `DELETE /products/{productRef}` (`:78-91`) | `SellerProductController.java:101-105` → `SellerProductService.java:390-432` | **IMPLEMENTED** — hard delete (rows + S3 objects), 409 when any offer has been ordered. Note the fixture/frontend describe DELETE as an **ARCHIVED soft delete** (`fixture.yaml:1823-1824`); the backend does a hard delete. |
  | `POST /products/{productId}/variants` → `SellerVariant` (`:533-556`) | `SellerProductController.java:61-66` → `SellerProductService.java:284-302` | **IMPLEMENTED** |
  | `PATCH /variants/{variantId}` → `SellerVariant` (`:727-752`) | `SellerProductController.java:80-84` → `SellerProductService.java:248-277` | **IMPLEMENTED** |
  | `DELETE /variants/{variantId}` (`:707-726`) | `SellerProductController.java:68-72` → `SellerProductService.java:311-352` | **IMPLEMENTED** (409 on last-variant and on sold-variant) |
  | `POST /products/{productId}/images/upload-url` (`:578-615`) | `SellerProductController.java:107-111` → `SellerProductService.java:484-542` | **IMPLEMENTED** (presign + PENDING row + 7-image cap + storage budget) |
  | `POST /products/{productId}/images/confirm` (`:655-684`) | `SellerProductController.java:113-116` → `SellerProductService.java:544-570` | **IMPLEMENTED** (HEADs the object, flips to STORED, records real size) |
  | `DELETE /images/{imageId}` (`:560-577`) | `SellerProductController.java:74-78` → `SellerProductService.java:355-372` | **IMPLEMENTED** |
  | `PUT /api/v1/products/{productId}/images/order` → `SellerImage[]` (`:616-654`, op `reorderProductImages`) | — | **MISSING.** No handler, no service method, no repository support. A repo-wide grep for `images/order`, `imageIds` and `reorder` in `backend/src/main/java` returns nothing. The frontend already calls it: `ProductFormPanel.tsx:632-653`. |
  | `GET /api/v1/sellers/me/products` → `SellerProductRowPage` (`:1486-1530`, op `sellerListProductsV1`, params `q`, `status`, `categorySlug`, `stockBelow`, `sort`, `page`, `size`) | — | **MISSING entirely.** This is the endpoint the products screen actually uses (`useSellerCatalog.ts:39-42`). Nothing in the backend serves it, and `SellerProductRow`'s `status`, `totalStock`, `priceFrom`, `priceTo`, `imageCount`, `updatedAt`, `brandName`, `productRef` fields (`fixture.yaml:2517-2534`) have no backend source. |
  
  ### 4.2 Product `status` (ACTIVE/DRAFT) — is it persisted?
  
  **No. There is no `status` anywhere in the backend.**
  
  - `backend/src/main/resources/db/migration/V5__create_product.sql` creates `product` with `id, seller_id, title, brand_name, description, category, search_vector, created_at` — **no `status` column, and no `updated_at` column**.
  - Later migrations touching `product` are `V14__create_category_and_link_product.sql` (adds `category_id`, drops `category`) and `V15__add_slug_to_product.sql` (adds `slug`). Neither adds `status` or `updated_at`.
  - A grep for `status` across `backend/src/main/resources/db/migration/` hits only `image.status` (`V8__create_image.sql:9`, `PENDING|STORED`) and `orders.status` (`V9__create_orders.sql:14`).
  - `backend/src/main/java/com/arkindustries/amezo/catalog/Product.java:34-71` has no status field and no `@UpdateTimestamp`.
  - A grep for `ProductStatus|DRAFT|ARCHIVED` across `backend/src/main/java` returns **zero** hits.
  
  So: a new Flyway migration (next free version is **V17**) adding `product.status` (with a CHECK for `ACTIVE|DRAFT|ARCHIVED` and a default of `ACTIVE`, matching `V8`/`V9`'s style) plus `product.updated_at` is required before any of the status/sort/`updatedAt` contract can be honoured. Meanwhile the frontend already sends `status` on create (`SellerAddProduct.tsx:172`) and on update (`ProductFormPanel.tsx:161`) — the backend silently drops it, since Jackson ignores unknown record components only if configured to; either way nothing persists it.
  
  ### 4.3 Image upload / confirm / delete / reorder
  
  - upload-url: **IMPLEMENTED** (`SellerProductService.java:484-542`) — 15-min TTL (`:64`), 7-image cap (`:67`, enforced `:494-501`), per-file and total storage caps (`:604-616`), signed `Content-Length`, 503 when object storage is unconfigured.
  - confirm: **IMPLEMENTED** (`:544-570`) — `HeadObject` verification, 409 `already-confirmed`, 409 `upload-not-found`.
  - delete: **IMPLEMENTED** (`:355-372`) — row + object, ownership resolved through product *or* variant parent, object deletion deferred to `afterCommit` (`:442-456`).
  - **reorder: MISSING.** `ImageRepository` (`backend/src/main/java/com/arkindustries/amezo/catalog/ImageRepository.java`) has no bulk position update and no `findByIdInAndProductId`. `Image.position` is written once at presign time (`SellerProductService.java:506`) and never changed afterwards. The contract requires: whole-list PUT, first id is the cover, a missing id or a foreign image is a **422**, response is the renumbered `SellerImage[]`.
  
  ### 4.4 Variants CRUD
  
  - Create (as part of product create): **IMPLEMENTED** (`SellerProductService.java:153-164`)
  - Add to existing product: **IMPLEMENTED** (`:284-302`), SKU uniqueness pre-checked (`:378-388`)
  - Update (label/sku on `variant`, price/stock on `offer`): **IMPLEMENTED** (`:248-277`)
  - Delete: **IMPLEMENTED** (`:311-352`) with two 409s — `last-variant` and `variant-has-orders`
  - Duplicate: no dedicated endpoint; the frontend composes it from add-variant (`ProductFormPanel.tsx:541-551`) — consistent with the design, which also treats duplicate as a client-side insert.
  
  ### 4.5 What the VIEW drawer needs that the backend cannot supply
  
  The VIEW drawer's three stat tiles and its "Active orders" list need data no endpoint returns:
  
  | Drawer need | Backend today |
  |---|---|
  | **"Open orders"** count per product | **MISSING.** No endpoint filters orders by `productId`. `SellerOrderController` (`backend/src/main/java/com/arkindustries/amezo/orders/SellerOrderController.java:30-33`) accepts only `status` + `Pageable`. |
  | **"Active orders"** list (shortId, buyer, `qty × variant`, PLACED/SHIPPED) per product | **MISSING.** No query exists. |
  | Price range / total stock per product (list *and* drawer) | **MISSING from the list endpoint** — `SellerProductSummaryResponse.java` carries only `variantCount`. Derivable per-product from the detail endpoint's variants (`SellerProductDetailResponse.variants`), which is what `ProductFormPanel.tsx:308-329` does. |
  | `updatedAt` ("Last updated") | **MISSING** — no `updated_at` column (see 4.2). `createdAt` is available. |
  | Image count on the list row | **MISSING** — `SellerProductSummaryResponse` carries `thumbnailUrl` only. |
  
  **Feasibility of the orders-per-product query:** the data is there. `OrderLine` (`backend/src/main/java/com/arkindustries/amezo/orders/OrderLine.java:46-53`) stores `product_id_snapshot`, `variant_id_snapshot` and `seller_id_snapshot`, and `OrderLineRepository` already has `findBySellerIdSnapshot` (`:15`) and a JPQL join to `Order` filtered by `productIdSnapshot` (`:36-42`, used by `OrderLinePurchaseService`). What is missing is a repository method + an `orders.api` query interface.
  
  **Architecture constraint to respect:** `backend/src/test/java/com/arkindustries/amezo/architecture/PackageBoundaryTest.java` fails the build if `catalog` imports `orders` internals. Cross-feature access must go through an `orders.api.*` interface, exactly as `OfferOrderHistoryQuery` does (`backend/src/main/java/com/arkindustries/amezo/orders/api/OfferOrderHistoryQuery.java`, implemented by `orders/OfferOrderHistoryService.java:19-21`, consumed at `SellerProductService.java:22`, `:75`, `:328`, `:406`). An "open orders for product X" feature needs a sibling `orders.api.ProductOpenOrdersQuery`.
  
  ### 4.6 Backend conventions to follow
  
  **Package layout** — feature-sliced under `com.arkindustries.amezo`:
  ```
  catalog/                 entities (Product, Variant, Offer, Image), repositories,
                           services, controllers — all package-private-ish internals
  catalog/dto/             request + response records
  catalog/api/             the ONLY cross-feature surface (ProductExistenceQuery,
                           OfferStockService, OfferSnapshot, …)
  orders/, reviews/, identity/   same shape
  common/                  exceptions (NotFoundException, ConflictException,
                           PayloadTooLargeException, StorageCapReachedException,
                           StorageUnavailableException), reference data
  config/                  SecurityConfig and other composition-root beans
  ```
  Enforced by `PackageBoundaryTest` (`src/test/java/com/arkindustries/amezo/architecture/PackageBoundaryTest.java:39-50`); features may only see each other through `<feature>.api`.
  
  **Controller style** — `@RestController`, constructor injection (no `@Autowired`, no Lombok on controllers), `@Valid @RequestBody`, `@PathVariable UUID`, return the DTO directly for 200 and `ResponseEntity.status(CREATED)` / `noContent()` otherwise. `SellerProductController` deliberately has **no** class-level `@RequestMapping` because its routes straddle `/sellers/me/products` and `/products/{id}` (`SellerProductController.java:36-49`).
  
  **DTO style** — Java `record`s in `catalog/dto/`, one file each, jakarta validation annotations on the components, and a class javadoc explaining the design decision. PATCH requests use **null-means-untouched** semantics with `@Pattern(regexp = ".*\\S.*")` so "absent" and "blank" differ (`UpdateProductRequest.java`, `UpdateVariantRequest.java`). Nested response records are declared inside their parent (`SellerProductDetailResponse.SellerImageResponse`).
  
  **Entities** — Lombok `@Getter @Setter @NoArgsConstructor @AllArgsConstructor @Builder`, `@GeneratedValue(strategy = GenerationType.UUID)`, `@CreationTimestamp` on `created_at` with `updatable = false` (`Product.java:27-70`).
  
  **Errors** — throw the typed `common.exception` classes; conflicts carry an RFC7807 `type` URI, e.g. `ConflictException(URI.create("https://api/errors/sku-taken"), "SKU already in use", "<detail>", List.of(new ConflictException.FieldError("sku", "already in use")))` (`SellerProductService.java:378-388`).
  
  **Authentication / authorisation of the seller** — two layers, both required:
  1. `SecurityConfig` (`backend/src/main/java/com/arkindustries/amezo/config/SecurityConfig.java`): `.requestMatchers("/sellers/me/**").hasRole("SELLER")` for **every** method (`:132-138`), plus explicit per-method rules for the id-keyed routes — `DELETE /products/*` (`:139`), `DELETE /variants/*`, `/images/*` (`:143`), `PATCH /products/**` (`:144`), `POST /products/*/variants`, `/products/*/images/upload-url`, `/products/*/images/confirm` (`:145-147`), `PATCH /variants/**`, `/images/**` (`:148`). A new route that is not matched falls through to `anyRequest().denyAll()` and 403s — the comment at `:132-137` records that exact bug. **A new `PUT /products/*/images/order` will need its own matcher.**
  2. `CurrentSeller.sellerId()` (`identity/api/CurrentSeller.java`, resolved by `identity/CurrentSellerResolver.java` from the `SessionCookieAuthenticationFilter` principal), then the private `ownedProduct(UUID)` guard (`SellerProductService.java:633-640`) which **throws `NotFoundException`, not a 403, when the product belongs to someone else** — an id is not a capability. Every write path calls it first; variant/image writes resolve up to the owning product before checking (`:252`, `:315`, `:362-368`).
  
  **Tests** (`backend/src/test/java/...`) — read `catalog/SellerProductApiTest.java`, `catalog/ProductDetailApiTest.java`, `support/Fixtures.java`, `architecture/PackageBoundaryTest.java`:
  - Integration-first: `@SpringBootTest @AutoConfigureMockMvc @Testcontainers` with a `static @Container @ServiceConnection PostgreSQLContainer<?> postgres = new PostgreSQLContainer<>("postgres:16-alpine")` (`SellerProductApiTest.java:52-59`). No slice tests, no mocked repositories.
  - `MockMvc` + raw JSON text blocks for bodies; assertions via `status()`, `jsonPath()` and AssertJ against the repositories afterwards (`:116-171`).
  - Auth is a real session cookie: `sessionCookieFor(seller)` builds a `Session` row and the `mp_session` cookie (`support/Fixtures.java:59+`, used at `:118`). Every endpoint also gets a "without a session returns 401" test (`:130-134`).
  - External boundaries are `@MockitoBean`: `S3Client` (`:100-106`) and `OfferOrderHistoryQuery` (`:108-114`), each with a comment saying why.
  - Fixtures: `Fixtures.categoryId(categoryRepository, "outdoor")` and `Fixtures.uniqueSlug("fixture")` because `product.category_id` is NOT NULL and `product.slug` is UNIQUE (`support/Fixtures.java:31-56`).
  - Test names are full sentences: `listMineOnlyReturnsTheCallingSellersProducts`, `creatingAProductWithNoVariantsFails`, `deletingAProductRemovesItsVariantsAndOffers`.
  - `static { System.setProperty("aws.accessKeyId"/"aws.secretAccessKey", ...) }` is required in any test class that exercises presigning (`:61-68`).
  
  ---
  
  ## Summary of the biggest gaps
  
  1. **No VIEW drawer exists** — the whole view mode (stat tiles, Active orders, read-only variants/description, meta footer, Edit/Delete action bar, `aria-label="Product details"`) is unbuilt; the drawer goes straight to the edit form.
  2. **Row click does not open anything**; "Add product" navigates to a separate page instead of opening the drawer in ADD mode; "Full page" opens a new tab and has no "Back to panel" return.
  3. **No compound-component drawer exists** — `DetailDrawer` is a 63-line fixed-shape wrapper with 6 props and no footer/mode/aria slots, and there is **no dot-notation compound precedent anywhere in the repo** to copy (closest: `ui/chart.tsx`'s context + flat sibling exports).
  4. **`ProductFormPanel` has three save boundaries where the design has one**, and has no footer bar, no `canSave` gating, no `footerNote`, no "Publish product" label, and uses a `Switch` where the design uses an Active/Draft segmented pill.
  5. **Backend: product `status` is not persisted at all** — no column, no entity field, no DTO field, no enum. `updated_at` is likewise absent.
  6. **Backend: image reorder (`PUT /api/v1/products/{productId}/images/order`) does not exist**, though the frontend already calls it.
  7. **Backend: `GET /api/v1/sellers/me/products` (the filtered/sorted row list the screen actually uses) does not exist**, and neither does any `/api/v1` prefix.
  8. **Backend: no way to fetch orders for a product** — the VIEW drawer's "Open orders" tile and "Active orders" list have no data source, though `order_line.product_id_snapshot` makes it straightforward behind a new `orders.api` query interface.

  ---
  
  ## 1. Backend — what I implemented
  
  ### Architecture decision: hosted in `orders`, not a new `metrics` feature
  
  The fact table is `order_line`, which is `orders`' own entity, and every figure is an aggregate of it. A separate `metrics` feature would have needed a **new `orders.api` surface exposing line-level money** — a far wider export than the one narrow read it actually needs from elsewhere (a product's title, slug, category, thumbnail). That read goes the other way through `catalog.api`, exactly as `SellerOrderService` already reaches catalog for line-item titles. Same boundary, same direction, one new interface instead of two — and no edit to `PackageBoundaryTest.FEATURES`, which also keeps merge risk down. **`PackageBoundaryTest` passes.**
  
  The catalog-side interface is `catalog.api.ProductCatalogSummaryQuery` (+ record `ProductCatalogSummary`), modelled on `ProductVariantSummaryQuery` — batched `Map<UUID, …>`. I implemented it in a **new** `catalog/ProductCatalogSummaryService.java` rather than adding a 4th interface to `ProductService`, deliberately: `ProductService` is a file Agent B may well be touching, and a new file is a zero-conflict edit.
  
  ### The three things that bite
  
  1. **Never joined `orders.seller_id`.** No query in `SellerMetricsRepository` touches the `orders` table at all. Every window scopes on `order_line.seller_id_snapshot`. Proved live: all six fixture orders had `seller_id IS NULL` and all three endpoints still returned the right seller's money.
  2. **`previousRevenue` / `previousTotals` are nullable and never coalesced.** The top-products query is a single `LEFT JOIN` between the current-window and previous-window aggregates; `prev.revenue` reaches the DTO untouched as a nullable `BigDecimal`. No `COALESCE`, no primitive `double`. Verified live: one product `null` (didn't sell then), another `0.0` (sold at price 0 then) **in the same response**.
     - **Judgment call you should know about:** the contract doesn't define when `SellerMetrics.previousTotals` is null, so I made it null when the previous window holds **no line at all** for that seller, exactly parallel to `TopProduct.previousRevenue`. Rationale in the DTO javadoc: `order_line` is the only evidence this endpoint has, and it cannot tell a quiet month from a month before the seller existed. Telling those apart would need `seller.created_at`, which is `identity`'s data behind the boundary. Returning zeros would report a measurement nobody took.
  3. **`views` / `conversionRate` have no source — returned `null`, per the settled decision.** No table, column or counter records a view anywhere. Made both nullable in `fixture.yaml` (`MetricTotals` **and** `MetricPoint`), backend returns `null`, frontend renders an explicit not-tracked state. **No hardcoded number, no view-tracking table, buyer app untouched.** `currency` is a documented constant `"USD"` (no price column in the schema carries one; `formatPrice` is fixed to USD too).
  
  ### Other decisions worth your eye
  - **Window semantics**: UTC calendar days, half-open `[from 00:00Z, to+1d 00:00Z)`. Previous window = equal-length span ending where the current one starts (contiguous, non-overlapping).
  - **`orders` = `COUNT(DISTINCT order_id)`, not line count.** A basket with three of a seller's products is one order to that seller; counting lines would also corrupt `averageOrderValue`.
  - **`share` is against the whole window's revenue**, not the returned rows. A top-5 whose shares summed to 100% would tell the seller those five are their entire shop.
  - **Empty bucket fill** is done in Java (`Bucket.startsWithin`), matching Postgres `date_trunc` — a chart drawn only from measured rows joins 1 Sep straight to 30 Sep.
  - **`to < from`** is treated as an empty window (zero totals, empty series, null previousTotals) rather than a 400. There is no `BadRequestException` in `common/exception` and adding one plus an `ApiExceptionHandler` method would be a second shared-file edit for a case outside the brief. Missing/malformed `from`/`to` already 400 via Spring (verified). **Unrecognised `interval` falls back to `day`** — it chooses a grouping, never which rows are counted.
  - **Migration `V19`** (V17/V18 left free for Agent B): composite index `(seller_id_snapshot, created_at)`. Equality column first so the range is an index scan. V10's single-column index left in place deliberately.

  `V17__add_status_and_updated_at_to_product.sql` — CHECK `ACTIVE|DRAFT|ARCHIVED`, default `ACTIVE`, in V8/V9's style; `updated_at` NOT NULL backfilled to `created_at` (not `now()`, which would claim every listing was edited the day this deployed); composite index `(seller_id, status)`. Applied cleanly — `\d product` showed both columns and `product_status_check`.
  
  Create with `status: "DRAFT"` now round-trips (it was silently dropped before):
  
  ```json
  {
    "id": "bac57e29-8ea0-450e-b12f-f7beca9d12bf",
    "slug": "bverify-status-probe",
    "title": "BVerify Status Probe",

    "updatedAt": "2026-09-26T15:38:41.763599Z"
  }
  ```
  
  PATCH semantics, all four verified:
  - `{"status":"ACTIVE"}` → `status= ACTIVE`
  - `{"title":"…Renamed"}` → `title= …Renamed status= ACTIVE` (null means untouched)
  - `{"status":"ARCHIVED"}` → **422** `https://api/errors/status-not-settable` — the contract reserves ARCHIVED for the soft delete, and the portal has no way back from it
  - `{"status":"BOGUS"}` → **400** at deserialization
  
  **Bug the curl found that compiling would not have.** The first PATCH returned `updatedAt` equal to `createdAt`, while the row in the database had moved. `@UpdateTimestamp` lands at flush, and `getMine()` reads the product out of the persistence context — its other queries touch `variant`/`image`, so Hibernate's auto-flush sees no overlap with the dirty product. The drawer that had just saved would show a stale "Last updated" until it refetched. Fixed with `saveAndFlush`; re-verified: `createdAt 15:38:41.763575Z`, `updatedAt 15:40:11.416127Z`, `MOVED: True`.
  
  ### 1b. `PUT /api/v1/products/{productId}/images/order`
  
  Whole-list PUT; position is the submitted index, so no transient duplicate position. Happy path (`c,a,d,b`) returned the renumbered `SellerImage[]` at positions 0–3, and `psql` confirmed the rows persisted in that order. All four refusals:
  
  ```
  missing id   → 422 invalid-image-order  "imageIds must list every image of this product. Missing: eeee…003, eeee…004."
  foreign id   → 422 invalid-image-order  "Not images of product aaaa…001: eeee-9999-…001."
  duplicate id → 422 invalid-image-order  "imageIds lists the same image more than once."
  empty list   → 422 validation-error     errors[{field:"imageIds", reason:"must not be empty"}]
  other seller → 404 (not 403)
  ```
  
  A partial list is refused rather than applied to the ids it names — the omitted images keep their old positions, which leaves two images at position 0.

  `imageCount: 3` against 4 image rows confirms PENDING uploads are excluded. Every parameter exercised:

Empty case returns `{0, 0, []}`.
  
  ### Security (minimal, for your merge with Agent A)
  
  `config/SecurityConfig.java` — **three lines of substance**, all additive:
  1. `.requestMatchers("/api/v1/sellers/me/**").hasRole("SELLER")` — the `/api/v1` twin of the existing `/sellers/me/**` rule. Needed because `/sellers/me/**` does **not** match `/api/v1/sellers/me/…`, so every new versioned route would 403 via `anyRequest().denyAll()` — the same trap `POST /sellers/me/products` fell into.
  2. `.requestMatchers(HttpMethod.PUT, "/api/v1/products/*/images/order").hasRole("SELLER")`
  3. CORS `setAllowedMethods` gained `"PUT"` — **this one is easy to miss in a merge.** It is the first route this API serves on PUT; without it the browser preflight is refused and the call never reaches the endpoint, and only from a browser, so it passes every curl and fails in the app.
  
  No existing matcher was reordered or changed.
  
  ### Flagged, not changed: DELETE is a hard delete
  
  The contract documents `DELETE /products/{productRef}` as a soft delete to `ARCHIVED`. The backend still hard-deletes (and 409s when the product has been ordered). **I did not change that behaviour.** V17's CHECK allows `ARCHIVED` so turning it into a soft delete later is a code change, not another migration, and the update endpoint refuses `ARCHIVED` so nothing can reach that state by the back door in the meantime. Your call whether to converge.
  
  ---
  
  ## 2. The shared drawer — API and why it's shaped that way
  
  `frontend/src/features/seller-portal/components/Drawer.tsx` (+ `drawerContext.ts`). Built on the existing shadcn `sheet.tsx`; **no new dependency**.
  
  ```tsx
  <Drawer open onOpenChange mode="view|edit|add" ariaLabel="Product details"
          width={520} expanded onExpandedChange>
    <DrawerHeader>            {/* header slot; expand + close are supplied */}
      <DrawerEyebrow/>        {/* status pill + category */}
      <DrawerTitle/> <DrawerSubline/>
    </DrawerHeader>
    <DrawerBody/>             {/* the only scroll container */}
    <DrawerFooter note={…}>   {/* note + actions */}
  </Drawer>
  ```
  
  **Flat named exports**, also attached as `Drawer.Header`/`Drawer.Body`/… — same functions, two spellings. There is no dot-notation precedent in this repo; every shadcn primitive exports flat, and `chart.tsx` is the closest idiom (a `createContext` shared between siblings, exported flat). I followed that rather than inventing a house style.
  
  **Why slots rather than props.** The parts vary independently — a view drawer has a status pill, three tiles and an action bar; a form drawer has a segmented status control and a save footer; an order drawer has neither. As props that is a growing list of optional `ReactNode`s, each one a decision the drawer makes on the caller's behalf. As slots the drawer owns only what is genuinely common.
  
  **Why expanding doesn't remount.** Expanding changes the panel's **classes only** — the Radix dialog stays mounted, so `children` keep their position in the React tree and in-progress form state survives both directions. Rendering the body in a different container when expanded would look identical and silently wipe the form. There is a test for this and a Playwright assertion.
  
  **Context stability.** The value is `useMemo`'d on `[mode, expanded, onExpandedChange, onOpenChange]`, so consumers of `useDrawer` don't re-render on unrelated parent renders.
  
  Two things worth knowing:
  - `ariaLabel` is carried by an `sr-only` `SheetTitle`, not `aria-label`. Radix names the dialog from its Title and `aria-labelledby` beats `aria-label` — my first attempt was silently ignored and the tests caught it. `DrawerTitle` is a plain `<h2>`, so the dialog announces "Product details" rather than the product's name.
  - shadcn's `SheetContent` always renders its own close button at `top-4 right-4`, where the expand control sits. The drawer hides it with CSS, so a real browser shows one; **jsdom compiles no Tailwind, so both are in the tree in tests** — `Drawer.test.tsx` documents this and targets the header's.
  
  **Migration: Products only,** as instructed. `DetailDrawer.tsx` is untouched (0 lines changed) and still used by `SellerOrders.tsx` and `SellerRefunds.tsx`. Follow-up work: both pair it with a standalone-route panel in exactly the shape `Drawer` takes, so they can move over unchanged whenever nothing else is in flight on those files.

None of this existed.
  - **Edit mode** swaps to a second drawer (`aria-label="Product form"`, 620px) — a mode swap, not a tab.
  - **Add product** opens the **same drawer in ADD mode**, with one footer save, `canSave` (complete AND dirty), the design's `footerNote` ("Still needs a title, a category, every variant field.") and label "Publish product". It no longer navigates away.
  - **Full page** expands in place with a reciprocal **Back to panel**; the Playwright run types a title, expands, and asserts the value survives both ways.
  - **De-duplicated the add form**: `SellerAddProduct.tsx` was a second copy of the form. It is now page chrome around the same `ProductCreateFields` + `useProductCreateForm` the drawer uses.
  - **List gaps closed**: count line "5 products · 5 active", flash strip after save/delete, the design's four sorts, stock pills (out / low / plain), row meta `<brand> · <N> images`, empty copy "No products match those filters" / "Clear the search or pick a different status.", default page size **5**.
  - **Row delete** kept with the design's compact inline confirm (Delete? / Yes / No), distinct from the drawer's Confirm delete / Keep.
  
  **"Archived" filter and "Updated" column — removed, as decided.** The Archived option could never match: nothing writes `ARCHIVED` (DELETE hard-deletes, update refuses it), so it filtered an always-empty set — it advertised a state the system can't reach. The Updated column isn't in the design and `updatedAt` now has a home in the drawer's meta footer; dropping it also buys back horizontal room on a 7-column table.
  
  ---
  
  ## 4. What remains missing, and why
  
  1. **`ProductFormPanel` still has three save boundaries** (product fields / per-variant / images) where the design has one footer save. This is the one design deviation I did not close. Variants and images are separate endpoints (`PATCH /variants/{id}`, `POST /products/{id}/variants`, `DELETE /variants/{id}`, the image upload trio), so one "Save changes" means sequencing N calls with partial-failure semantics — a rewrite of that 905-line file and its test suite, not a wiring change, and high risk of regressing the 511-test baseline. The edit drawer's footer **says so** ("Each section above saves on its own.") rather than showing a dead Save button. ADD mode does have the design's single gated save, so the pattern is in place to extend.
  2. **Orders and Refunds still use the old `DetailDrawer`** — deliberate, per your instruction.
  3. **DELETE is still a hard delete**, flagged above, not silently converted.
  4. **Backend integration tests were written but could not be executed here** — see verification below.
  
  Nothing was invented for want of data. The one place I added contract surface (`ProductOpenOrders` + the open-orders path in `fixture.yaml`, and `createdAt`/`updatedAt` on `SellerProductDetail`) was because the design needs those fields and the backend now really returns them.

I did widen one thing and want to flag it: **I told it to build `PATCH` as well as the `GET` you named.** The `seller` table has no store fields at all, so a read-only endpoint would return an empty profile for every seller and leave Store Settings still broken — they're one entity and one service, so splitting them would be artificial. Say the word if you'd rather have GET alone.

**Low-stock SKU** needed the ambiguity resolved before it could be built, so I made a call rather than bouncing it back. `sku` is a column on `variant`, so a product with three variants has three SKUs and a bare `sku` field on a product row would lie about what it is. The row will name **the variant with the least stock**, as a self-describing nullable object. That's honest about the schema and is what the widget is for — a seller reading "Low stock" needs to know *which* variant to restock, and "restock this product" isn't actionable when four of five variants are fine. If you wanted literally the design's single SKU string, tell me and I'll redirect.

Two things I carried forward from the last round rather than repeating the pain:

- **Both agents get an explicit step 0** to `git reset --hard` onto my branch. The harness cuts worktrees from `main`, which is behind — Agent D would otherwise have had no `SellerProductRow` to extend, since Agent B only created it this session.

  # Agent C — Seller store profile backend
  
  Worktree synced first (`git reset --hard origin/claude/busy-noether-dmy03s`, HEAD = `3b941b4` "Persist product status, add the missing product endpoints, and a shared drawer"). Nothing committed, nothing pushed — 12 new files, **zero modified files**.
  
  ## What I built, and why both halves
  
  `GET` and `PATCH /api/v1/sellers/me/store`, backed by a new `seller_store` table (V18). **I built both, as instructed, and I'd have argued for it anyway:** a read endpoint alone would have given every seller a store they could see and never fill in — the Store Settings form would load, show the auto-provisioned defaults, and fail on save. They are one entity, one service, one set of PATCH rules; the read is literally what the write returns (`updateMine` maps the same `SellerStore` the save flushed). Splitting them would have meant shipping a screen that is still broken.
  
  ### Placement: `identity`, no ArchUnit change
  `seller_store` is a 1:1 extension of the row `identity` already owns, its FK is `seller.id`, and auto-provisioning reads `seller.full_name` / `seller.email` to derive the default. A new feature would have needed a new `identity.api` query just to get those back, plus a `FEATURES` entry in `PackageBoundaryTest`. `PackageBoundaryTest` passes unchanged.
  
  I did **not** reuse `catalog.Slugs` for the handle — it's another feature's internals (ArchUnit forbids it) and it answers a different rule (200 chars, any shape). `identity.StoreHandles` implements `StoreHandle`'s own rule instead.
  
  ### Auto-provisioning — a store exists for every seller
  First read of a seller with no `seller_store` row creates one; there is no 404 path. The name is **derived from real data, never invented**: `seller.full_name` if the account has one, otherwise the local part of the signup email. In practice `full_name` is always null today (V1 documents that nothing captures it), so the email local part is the branch that runs. The dashboard's `<h1>` now shows e.g. `Northwind Supply` or `ada.lovelace09` instead of always falling back to `'Dashboard'`, and the seller renames it in Store Settings. I did not consider `product.brand_name` — it's `catalog` internals, per-product and nullable.
  
  `SellerStoreProvisioner` is a separate bean with `@Transactional(REQUIRES_NEW)` for one specific reason: a lost insert race (two tabs on a seller's first visit) inside the caller's transaction would mark it rollback-only, so the caller could not then read the row the winner wrote. Its own transaction rolls back alone; `requireStore` catches `DataIntegrityViolationException`, re-reads, and retries once more for the rarer case where the **handle** rather than `seller_id` collided.
  
  ### `updatedAt` — checked, not assumed
  `updateMine` uses `saveAndFlush` **and maps the instance it returns**, not the one it mutated. Two separate traps there: `@UpdateTimestamp` lands at flush (Agent B's bug), and when the store was provisioned moments earlier in the provisioner's own transaction, the instance in hand is *detached*, so `save` merges and the timestamp lands on the merged copy. Verified against the row itself, below.
  
  ### 409 and validation
  `handle-taken` is modelled exactly on `SellerProductService`'s `sku-taken`: `ConflictException` with a type URI plus `FieldError("handle", "already in use")`, which `ApiExceptionHandler` renders as RFC7807 with an `errors` array. `StoreSettings.tsx` already looks for `errors[].field === 'handle'`. Re-submitting the store's *own* handle is not a conflict.
  
  `StoreHandle`'s pattern, minLength and maxLength are enforced server-side on the DTO **and** repeated as a `CHECK` constraint in V18 — the column is what a manual `UPDATE` or a second writer has to get past.
  
  ### PATCH semantics
  Repo convention, as instructed: **null = untouched** (`@Pattern(".*\\S.*")` on `name`, not `@NotBlank`, so "leave the name alone" stays expressible). Beyond that, **blank = clear to null** for the optional fields, because `StoreSettings.tsx` sends every optional field on every save including `""` — clearing a tagline *is* a save of `""`, and rejecting it would make "remove my tagline" impossible.

  Second GET for A returns the same `id` and the same `updatedAt` — provisioned once, not per read.
  
  **Colliding default handles de-duplicate** (`Bits And Bobs` / `Bits and bobs`):

  Step 0 done first: worktree reset to `origin/claude/busy-noether-dmy03s` @ `3b941b4` "Persist product status, add the missing product endpoints, and a shared drawer". `SellerProductRow` and `GET /api/v1/sellers/me/products` were present before I touched anything.
  
  ## The contract field
  
  `SellerProductRow.lowestStockVariant` — nullable, `$ref` to a new `LowestStockVariant` component:
  
  ```yaml
  LowestStockVariant:
    type: object
    required: [id, label, sku, stockQty]
    properties:
      id: { type: string, format: uuid }
      label: { type: string }
      sku: { type: string }
      stockQty: { type: integer }
  ```
  
  ```yaml
  lowestStockVariant:
    allOf: [{ $ref: '#/components/schemas/LowestStockVariant' }]
    nullable: true
  ```
  
  Shaped that way for four reasons, all documented in the yaml beside the schema:
  
  1. **Named, not `sku`.** `sku` is a column on `variant`. A `sku` string on the product row would be null for every product past its first variant, and the rows where it *was* set would be misdescribing what they named. The field name says what it is.
  2. **It carries `label` as well as `sku`** because "AGD-SCARCE" alone is not a thing a seller recognises; the label is the human half of the same identity, and the drawer/product screens already print them together.
  3. **It carries its own `stockQty`**, deliberately separate from the row's `totalStock`. Those are different numbers — on my live fixture the tied product reads `totalStock: 9` while the named variant holds `3`. A widget pairing the sku with `totalStock` would tell the seller to order the wrong quantity. My frontend test asserts exactly that pair.
  4. **Nullable, not an empty object.** A product with no variants, or with variants but no offer, still reaches a low-stock list (its `totalStock` is 0), so "there is no line to name" is a real answer the row has to be able to give.
  
  ## Tie-break
  
  **`(stockQty ASC, sku ASC)`.** `sku` carries a `UNIQUE` constraint across the whole `variant` table (`V6__create_variant.sql`), so that pair is a *total* order — the pick is genuinely stable, not merely usually stable, and no third key is needed. Verified live over 5 consecutive requests: always `AGD-TIE-A`, even though `AGD-TIE-B` was inserted first.
  
  The MSW mock uses `<`/`>` on the sku rather than `localeCompare`, so it orders by code unit exactly as Java's `String.compareTo` does. A locale-aware collation would disagree with the API on precisely the rows this field exists to stabilise.
  
  ## Backend: no new query, no N+1, no second endpoint
  
  `listRows` already batch-loads the page's variants (`findByProductIdIn`) and their offers (`findByVariantIdIn`) — it needs them for `variantCount`, `totalStock` and the price range. The pick is a pure fold over data that is **already in memory**, so it costs **zero** additional round trips. That is strictly better than extending the native SQL, which would have added a second LATERAL for data already fetched.
  
  Proved with `LOGGING_LEVEL_ORG_HIBERNATE_SQL=DEBUG`, counting statements per request:
  
  | request | products on page | SQL statements |
  |---|---|---|
  | `?stockBelow=10&size=5&sort=stock_asc` | 3 | **6** |
  | `?size=50` (unfiltered) | 4, 7 variants | **6** |
  
  The six: session lookup (auth), the `searchMine` native page query, then `variant … WHERE product_id IN (?,?,?)`, `offer … WHERE variant_id IN (?,?,?,?)`, `image … WHERE product_id IN (…)`, `category`. Constant in the page size — no per-product lookup.
  
  `listMine` is byte-for-byte unchanged (`git diff -U0 … | grep -c listMine` → 0). `SecurityConfig.java` untouched. **No migration** — this is a query-shape change only, so V20 was not needed.
  
  ## Real curl output (live Postgres, my own DB `amezo_agent_d`, my own port 8087)
  
  Seeded two sellers; four products for me, one for a rival holding the lowest stock figure in the table. `GET /api/v1/sellers/me/products?sort=title_asc`:
  
  ```json
  { "title": "A Three Variants",      "totalStock": 49,
    "lowestStockVariant": { "id": "d2000000-…-000000000012", "label": "Scarce", "sku": "AGD-SCARCE", "stockQty": 2 } }
  ```
  Three variants at 40 / 2 / 7, inserted highest-first. Picks the 2. ✔
  
  ```json
  { "title": "B Tied On Stock",       "totalStock": 9,
    "lowestStockVariant": { "id": "d2000000-…-000000000022", "label": "First",  "sku": "AGD-TIE-A",  "stockQty": 3 } }
  ```
  Three variants all at 3, inserted `TIE-B`, `TIE-A`, `TIE-C`. Picks `TIE-A` — the rule, not insertion order. Repeated 5×, identical every time. ✔
  
  ```json
  { "title": "C No Variants At All",  "variantCount": 0, "totalStock": 0, "lowestStockVariant": null }
  { "title": "D Variants No Offers",  "variantCount": 1, "totalStock": 0, "lowestStockVariant": null }
  ```
  Both null shapes. ✔
  
  **Scoping:** `"totalElements": 4` — the rival's `E Someone Elses` (variant `AGD-RIVAL`, stock **1**, lower than anything of mine) never appears, and is never named. Signing in as the rival returns `totalElements: 1` with only `AGD-RIVAL`. A competitor's SKU and stock position cannot leak into this row. ✔
